# CLAUDE.md — project handoff

You are joining the 8hp-tcu-open project mid-flight. This file is your orientation: what this is, where it stands, how work flows here, and the rules that exist because breaking them cost real bench time. Read it fully before touching anything. The git log's commit messages are detailed engineering records — the project's real history lives there and in `~/tcu_sweep/`.

## What this is

An open C reimplementation of the firmware for the Bosch transmission control unit of the ZF 8HP (P/N 1034420267, Renesas SH7254R, SH-2A big-endian, superloop, no RTOS). The stock firmware is fully decompiled (10,982 functions, Ghidra sweep, specs 19–26 in `docs/spec/`); our rewrite lives in `firmware/` and boots, walks, and converses with the supervisor chip on the real bench. The goal: **raise the solenoid outputs to the engaged rail (~1.77 A) under open-firmware control** — the stock does it in under half a second from power-on; our firmware has not yet been granted it. Everything else (M5 limp, M6 shifting, M7 Nissan integration) follows the rail.

## The one-sentence state (as of 2026-11-11 close)

The bench runs r02a; the walk tick is alive at stock cadence; the supervisor chip's certification gate (`0x14` bit 1) has refused six wire-law hypotheses; the mechanical transcript tool caught that our per-pass word SEQUENCE was reversed vs the stock — fixed in commit f716fd7 — and five smaller deltas are enumerated. **The chip's gate is flash-reachable (the stock opens it every boot on this same bench) — never accept "the wire can't do it."**

**CORRECTION (2026-11-11 evening, supersedes the earlier close):**
- **r02a is in a restart loop, and has been since it was flashed** [O]. Passive 0x7F0 capture (90 s, 919 frames): every frame strictly alternates boot-init (`val=3F walk=00 mode=00`) / just-armed (`val=06 walk=07 mode=07`), never a third frame from one boot — app life 105–205 ms. `s_now_ms` reads alias at a ~196 ms period. The restart lands within ~10 ms of the silence window releasing [I]. `s_wovf_count` flat at 50 across ~170 restarts and no exception-announce frames — not the MCU WDT, not a CPU exception; the supervisor's reset line is the lead [I].
- **"The reset loop is dead" was wrong for r02a; the silence-shape "refutation" was not clean** (the chip never got a steady conversation); **the "FSM-advance wedge" does not exist** — on the host the FSM leaves supervise at 3.705 s; DUTY=0 on r02a was the restarts wiping the counters. The r02a witness log already showed it: 51 of 90 rounds at init values (`st84=00 REQ=00 r14=00`).
- **The stock has NO batch silence** [O, decode]: `0x8EE82` is entry 26 of the 5 ms task's mode-0 list (row 7 col 0, list `0x42CD8`, task object `0x41378`); the OS tick (CMT 2.5 ms, `0x91A30(0x186A,0x19)`) starts on the 2nd main-loop pass (`0x7DAA4` → `0xFFF93B8C` → `0x6F144` → `0xFFF93B40` → `0x67A7C`). The state-3 gate `0x67B16` holds only the background list (row 0). Spec 21's "+95–105 ms" row is the first variant-bank pass, not the batch start. `first500ms_diff.md`'s `0x45D24…`/`0x7FE40` are ELF file offsets — flash addresses are `0x44D24…`/`0x7EE40`.
- **r02b (f716fd7 as built) must not be flashed** — it keeps the silence gate and would restart identically. **r02c** = pool-order fix + dwell stamp, batch silence gate removed (monitor lead-in unchanged): built, `build/r02c_IFla.bin`, NOT flashed.
- **The heartbeat's uptime bytes always read 0** on these builds (`s_tick_count` is not advanced on the CMT path) — use the cell bytes as the restart detector.
- **THE STOCK'S TASK RATES (decoded 2026-11-11 late; table base `0x43430`, 10 rows × 6 modes; rows 8/7/6/5/4/3/2 = 2.5/5/10/20/30/50/100 ms tasks, row 9 = per-mode init list, row 0 = background):**
  - **The monitor `0x873F0` runs every 2.5 ms, in every mode, from the first OS tick** [O]: stub `0x7EE2E` (`jmp 0x873F0`) is entry 2 of the 2.5 ms list in modes 0–3. Corroborated twice: the monitor supervises its own period (9 passes must span ≤ 25 000 µs, cal `0x1919FA`=9 / `0x1912D6`=0x61A8, else fault 0x223), and the stock driving RAM dump holds 2506 µs in the span cell `0xFFF934C0`. No lead-in exists in the function (walk task, one-shot and outer FSM run unconditionally every pass). **Ours: 5 ms, after a 20-pass lead-in — both wrong.** Pass-count budgets in `sup_walk.c` assume 5 ms passes.
  - **The batch `0x8EE82` runs at 5 ms only in mode 0, then at 10 ms** [O]: mode 0 → 5 ms list (reply-proc `0x8EE52`@5, J2 stage `0x8EDF6`@24, F stage `0x8EE30`@25, batch@26). Modes 1–3 → the 10 ms list (`0x8EE02`@2, reply-proc@3, `0x8EE12`@70, `0x8EE80`@71, F stage@75, batch@76), while `0x8EDEA` and the J2 stage `0x8EDF6` stay in the 5 ms list. **Ours: 5 ms forever.**
  - **The mode switch** [O]: `0x6F0B4` (5 ms list) requests via `0x784B4`/`0x6F0FC` — from mode 0 the request is 1 once startup module 2 (`0xFFF93B81`) reads 0x0F (`0x6BC44`); `0x66E06` applies pending `0xFFF93B34` → `0xFFF848CF` and `0xFFF93B33` and runs the new mode's row-9 list. `0xFFF848CF != 0` is also the boot-state 2→3 condition, so **state 3 ⇔ mode ≥ 1**. Stock driving RAM dump: state 3, mode 1, startup modules B7E/B81/B85/B86/B87 = 0x0F. **When** [O, decoded while porting]: module 2 ← module 1 (`0xFFF93B7E`) ← modules 7 and 11. Module 11 (`0xFFF93B85`) is set by the mode sequencer `0x85160` when the selftest sub-state `0xFFF9500D` reads 0x40 (ladder complete — which needs the gate bit) or when its retry countdown `0xFFF9414D` expires [length not decoded]; module 7 (`0xFFF93B86`) by the seq2 FSM `0x85828` on a channel-status mask. **So the 5 ms → 10 ms batch switch happens AFTER certification** — through the gate window the stock is in mode 0 with the batch at 5 ms, as ours is. `0x92E80` (under the J2 stage `0x8EDF6`) QUEUES J2, so in mode 1 J2 stays on the wire at 5 ms while F/J6/J1/J5/J3/J4 go to 10 ms.
  - **r02d (built, NOT flashed, `build/r02d_IFla.bin`, app CRC `E8AF65C2`)** = r02c + the monitor at the stock's cadence (two scheduler entries per 5 ms → 2/3 ms alternation, 2.5 ms mean) with the lead-in removed; pass-count budgets doubled to keep their durations (durations still [I]); and a port fix the faster pass exposed — the staging confirm now has its own freshness mark (`s_pp_cnt_at_stage`) because the walk task's reply processor consumed every fresh reply before the confirm saw it (the era never committed, and the F1 timeout then flipped the walk back to level mode ~0.4 s into the duty era). Host trace: `8406` at pass 1, era committed and ask at ~10 ms, FSM leaves supervise at 3.605 s, duty mode holds in both echo models. **The post-certification 10 ms batch split is decoded but NOT ported.** r02c is superseded — do not flash it.

**ADDENDUM (2026-11-11 night, Claude Opus 5.5 session — supersedes r02d as the next flash):**
- **The stock never sends J7** [O, three lines]: descriptor `0x40BC0` has no literal anywhere in flash (every other bus-B descriptor has one, in its queuer's pool); the stock driving RAM dump holds J7's TX/RX staging `0xFFF885E0..E3 = 0000 0000` while J4's neighbour is live (`00FF/119F`); the handler-completeness check `0x93126 → 0x931F8(&0x90973, …, mask 7)` expects bits 0–2 only — J7's handler `0x92CC8` owns bit 3 (dump: `0x90973 = 07`). r01…r02d sent `NIB:0000` every 2nd pass — a word the chip never hears from the stock. **Removed** (descriptor/handler kept dead, as in the stock).
- **Delta (a) F2 position is stock-true, not a delta** [O ring/priority; I timing, strong]: the queue engine `0x937F8` keeps per-bus normal (10) and priority (5) rings, selected by descriptor byte 1; dispatcher `0x93902` drains priority first. F (`0x40C30`) is priority 0 → tail insert; J1/J5 are queued in the same CPU burst, well before J6 completes → F2 after J5, as ours. opening_200ms's "[F2] before J1" placement is refuted.
- **Delta (b) walk interleave is a transcript-model artifact**: the only priority-1 bus-B descriptors are the walk `0x40A14` (4f, staging `0x88120`, cb `0x911D8`) and the 7-frame `0x409F4` (cb `0x910EE`) — the same structure as our `JOBQ_WALK`. Their wire position is the asynchronous tick's.
- **Delta (c) closed** (J7 above). Delta (d) closed in r02d. **Open: (e) duty tokens** (post-supervise, ~3.6 s — outside the stock's gate window).
- **The transcript tool now judges**: it previously truncated at P78 (2048-word recorder) and always exited 0. Now: 8192 words, overflow fails, the init J5 launches via `sup_job_engine_pump()` as main.c does, and every pass P01–P100 must carry exactly `J4, J3, J2, F, J6, J1, J5, F2` (walk-queue jobs excluded). Negative-tested: the r02d batch fails at "P02 slot 2: got NIB".
- **r02e** = r02d + J7 removed: `build/r02e_IFla.bin`, one block vs r02d, app CRC `3212EC5D`, host 21/21. NOT flashed. **r02d superseded — do not flash it.** Prediction: as r02d (no restarts [I]; `8406` + ask in ~15 ms; FSM leaves supervise ~3.6 s); **the gate: no prediction** — but this is the first build whose cyclic bus-B job set and order match the stock's on every pass, asserted mechanically.

App life between restarts: r01r ~30–60 s (U6 sawtooth to 6121, GRANT seen); r01s a few s (NIB 313→93→396; walk tick dead); r01t minutes (DUTY 51198→32172); r01u ~1.5 s and r01v ~1 s (REQ/ONESHOT toggling 01/00, DUTY ≤ ~350); **r01w none — NIB 437→4144 monotonic over 150 s; the only build with neither the walk nor the ask on the wire**; r01x/r02a ~100 ms (42/65, 62/90 init rounds); r01y short (REQ toggles 11/90); r02d/r02e ~40–55 ms. `s_wovf_count` flat in every log that read it (150 all morning, 50 at r02a, 0x36 at r02e) → never the MCU WDT; our own `plat_reset` and the exception handler both end in a WDT overflow, so **the reset is external to the MCU** (the supervisor's reset line [I]; the SH7254R is under a steel plate — RES not probeable). **r02f — THE STOCK WALK CORE, PORTED (2026-11-11 night; built, NOT flashed, `build/r02f_IFla.bin`, app CRC `5AF4B18A`, one block vs r02e; NOT committed at time of writing)** — from `~/tcu_sweep/supervisor_reset_decode.md` (subagent decode, citations re-checked against the decompiles this session):
- **The walk core is now function-for-function** `0x6C12C/0x6C594/0x6C542/0x6C1EA/0x6C742/0x6C48C/0x6C0BA/0x6BF9C/0x6C720/0x6C802/0x6C63E/0x8D136/0x8D108/0x8D020/0x6C1BA` + the `0x873F0` body. Every walk job is a one-shot timed from the last W1/W2 completion stamp (`0x90544 = TCNTA − 0x29`, now set by our handlers); state 8 runs the `0x8D020` law from its first idle pass (token ctx 0x1E while reported > target 5, `84` resend at ≤ 5, resend on attention 7). **C2's 3.6 s dwell was a units error** (TCNTA is µs: `0x4B0+0x960` = the 06 era's 3600 µs CYCLE). The 8→0x12 exit is not ported (undecoded) — FSM holds 8.
- **Verified laws:** guard formula subtracts **0xAB (171)** — the old `walk_reply_window()` used 136; 0xAB reproduces the driving dump's `0x9383E..44 = 1CB2 1EEE 237F 1619` for the 1F era. The level programmer's normal delay is the 5 µs floor (`fire − 1 − elapsed`); shots land at `fire − 1`.
- **The DCNTD00 scale, settled from old bench data:** one tick (bits 31:8) ≈ 28 µs in our clock setup (r01t: 4 ticks ≈ 112 µs/launch; r01u: 0xB0 ticks ≈ 4.9 ms) — the "9.1 MHz burst-limited" reading was the confound. The shot is therefore closed-loop on TCNTA: armed short at 32 µs/tick, re-armed while > 40 µs remain, last stretch busy-waited in the walk ISR (`plat_walktmr_wait_until`). The ISR never self-re-arms.
- **Also fixed:** the job engine lock (stock `0x9665C(0x60)`: SR.I raised to 6 around check-free/queue/dispatch); the C9 alias (frame 1 now `C900`, not `0000`); the byte reply counters (`0x9054C/D`, `0x93B48/49` were overlapping u16s); W1/W2 echo is the full byte; FE13 view modes 2/3 exempt from the walk gate.
- **Transcript tool now judges timing** (10 µs-stepped, main.c schedule, stub one-shot at 28 µs/tick): tokens at stamp +2345/+3206 (stock `fire−1`), no 84 in (3206, 3774), no W2 runs > 4. Output: 100 W1 (all in slot) / 100 W2 alternating. Negative-tested (window skew → FAIL). **The first post-reply job is a RESEND, not a token** (the bench's first reply 6 vs expected 7 → attention 7) — the decode's verdict 3 was wrong on this point. Host 21/21; sh2a green.
- **PREDICTION (rule 1), r02f:** [I, ~50%] **no restarts** — `s_now_ms` (0xFFF95600) and TCNTA grow past seconds; J1 OK bits (0x907C8 & 0x22) appear [high]; walk alternates W1/W2 and `0x93BA1` sits 5↔6 [model-dependent]. **The gate: no prediction.** Three wire changes ride together (walk law, C9 `C900`, the lock) — r02e is the clean-refuted baseline for all three. **Named risks:** the token VALUE (delta (e), `sup_token_compute` vs the stock's 0x7EE8A sweep) is unverified — correct timing with a wrong token may still reset; the chip-side law is [I]. **If it still restarts ≤ 0.2 s:** next is the token value (decode 0x7EE8A against the dump's `0x92414`), then the ask (r02f with the one-shot disabled — one variable).
- **Witness plan:** `meas 0x02`, then `s_now_ms` 0xFFF95600, TCNTA 0xFFFFF220, `s_wovf_count` 0xFFF9E5DC, `s_pair_faults` 0xFFF95858, USE[DUTY]/USE[LEVEL] 0xFFF94226/28, `0x93BA1..A7`, `0x907C8..CA`, `0x9417D/E`. **(Superseded by the REVIEW bullet below — addresses moved; CRC 5AF4B18A superseded.)**
- **INDEPENDENT REVIEW (subagent, `~/tcu_sweep/r02f_review.md`):** all ported functions checked equivalent (0x6C12C, 0x6C594, 0x6C542, 0x6C1EA, 0x6C742, 0x6C48C incl. the 0x6BD48 register args, 0x6C0BA/0x6BF9C arithmetic, 0x6C720, 0x6C802, 0x6C63E, 0x8D136 with base `*(u8*)0x191A1B`=3, 0x8D108, 0x8D020, the 0x873F0 order, 0x6C1BA, 0x910EE/0x911D8, the 0x9665C lock — stock walk IPR is 6, so the raise-to-6 lock masks it as the stock's does); cell map clean (107 cells, no overlap, widths match). **One BLOCKER, fixed:** the stock's 5 µs delay floor rounded up to one ~28 µs DCNTD00 tick, so a resend programmed at e ∈ (CLOSE−28, CLOSE] launched inside the 84 keep-out — now delays ≤ 40 µs bypass the counter (spin on TCNTA + send at once). **Fixed too:** the arm tail runs with SR.I = 0xF as stock 0x91004 does; the per-pass token restage removed (stock writes 0x92414 only in 0x6C802). **The transcript now jitters** the task phases (seeded, 0–900 µs) over 400 frames and measures offsets from the firmware's own era base: 376 tokens all in slot, 372 resends, none in the keep-out; **negative-tested** — re-introducing the tick rounding → keep-out FAILs at +3211/+3221. Not fixed (NITs, unreachable in r02f or diagnostic): see the review file. Review finding 4 stands as a stated risk: three wire changes ride together (walk law, C9 `C900`, the lock) — C9's `0000` was carried by the clean r01w, so C9 alone is not expected to reset; `C900` is the stock's word.
- **r02f FINAL: app CRC `B29368C7`, one block vs r02e. Witness addresses (nm, this build):** `s_now_ms` **0xFFF95608**, TCNTA 0xFFFFF220, `s_wovf_count` **0xFFF9E5E4**, `s_pair_faults` **0xFFF95860**, RSPI vector count `s_vec_count[2]` **0xFFF955AC**, walk ISR count `s_walk_isr_count` **0xFFF955A4** and polled-fallback count `s_walk_poll_count` **0xFFF955A0** (the ISR must carry the walk — a polled walk stamps late), USE[DUTY]/USE[LEVEL] 0xFFF94226/28, `0x93BA1..A7`, `0x907C8..CA`, `0x9417D/E`.

**r02g — THE BASELINE FIXES (2026-11-12; built, NOT flashed, `build/r02g_IFla.bin`, app CRC `FF43FC78`, one block vs r02f; NOT committed at time of writing)** — four read-only reviewers (`~/tcu_sweep/baseline_review_{protocol,transport,platform,walkdata}.md`, consolidated `baseline_review_SUMMARY.md`), the fixes, then two independent re-reviews (`r02g_review_transport.md`, `r02g_review_protocol.md`) whose findings are applied:
- **B1 — completions never chained (r01s..r02f) [O]:** `plat_rspi_busy` returned busy across the burst-end handoff, so every reply handler's engine pump launched nothing; J1 (6th per pass) essentially never reached the wire (r02f `j1=00 50 00`). Now busy = channel state; the handoff window is `plat_rspi_owned`, read only by the stale-burst recovery. Host test negative-tested (stub with the old rule fails 6 checks).
- **B2 — the walk counter scale is MEASURED:** `plat_walktmr_calibrate` times DCNTD00 against TCNTA at boot (64 ticks, 1024 if fast; cap on spins) → `s_walk_q8` (µs/tick ×256; 0x100 = the 1 µs the clock tree predicts, 0x1C00 = the old 28 µs). Arm aims (20 µs + 1/32) short, 32-bit only. Transcript runs at 1 µs AND 28 µs with +1.5 % calibration error and 15 µs ISR latency; a storm verdict catches the old ÷32 arm (12 220 ISR entries).
- **Transport:** RSPI superloop polls removed (S1, the double-harvest race); OVRF → one retry then an error completion (frames = 0) through each handler's stock not-'@' branch (S2, 0x965E4/0x93A74); dead SPTEF time-bounded (200 µs) and completed by a 1 ms reaper in check-free — never under a running RX body, never the lab job, no recursion (S3).
- **Protocol:** J3 before J4 (S4, 0x91C12/0x91C16 — the corpus row was wrong, correction appended to `first500ms_diff.md`); handlers re-ported 1:1 to the stock bit map — A8 triple 0x907C8/C9/CA (J5 1, J1 2, J6 4, J2 0x10, F 0x20, J3 0x40), AA triple 0x90973/74/75 (J5 1, J1+C9 2, J6 4), the 0x93126/0x931F8 summarizer with per-round clear into 0x90971/72 (S6); J6 never class-checks rx[4] (S5); every queuer is check-free → stage → clear last rx → queue (S7); echoes compared against the SENT word; ECHO_24 unconditional (S8). J1's C9 echo compares rx[3] against the C9 DATA byte 0xFFF88427 [O, flash 0x92DD6/0x92DDE — the decompile's 0x88426 is the word base].
- **Platform:** `sup_pfc_mux` removed (S9: PG14/15 stay GPIO, PGCR2 = 0x00AA as the stock's 0x9A148 check requires); the stock's init-time GPIO drives ported INSTRUCTION-FOR-INSTRUCTION from the flash bytes (S10: 0x8FB96 incl. its **PC15 high pulse** over 150 PCPR reads — the Ghidra text folds it away; 0x90D12 PC10 = 1 after 75 PCDR RMWs; 0x91852; 0x67350) in an app-only `plat_app_init` (the loader shares `plat_init`; it changed only by S9's mux removal and is not part of the flashed app block); the unconditional CMT poll removed (S13).
- **Witness (S11):** `.noinit` `s_rst` — raw WTCR/WTCNT/WTSR/**WRCR** at entry BEFORE `wdt_init`, boot counter since power-on, previous boot's last-alive breadcrumb (TCNTA + code: 01 superloop, 02 monitor, 05 batch, 06 walk ISR, 1xx job xx done); `s_open[4][16]` — the first 16 bus-B completions of boots 1, 2, 3 and the current boot (raw tx0/rx). **WRCR & 0x80 clear ⇒ RES-pin/power-on reset [M §14.3.4].**
- **Transcript opening:** the fresh-chip reply is F0 (r02e m+1.0), not "level 6" — walk job #2 is the **selector-0 token** (88FF 880F 88F0 8800 …), landing at +3206 under main.c's phases (0x6BF9C picks CLOSE when programmed past OPEN). **THE LETTERS / REQUEST LAW (2026-11-12, `~/tcu_sweep/letters_law_decode.md`, subagent; core claims validated this session against the bytes, decompiles and the driving dump):**
- **The stock sends 0x9020 with 0x14 bit 1 = 0 — on purpose, every boot [O request writes; I wire].** Request cells 0xFFF939B6[ch] are written ONLY by ladder phases 1, 8, 0x40, 0x80 through 0x85E64, which lands the write only while the seq2 state 0x94FD0[ch] is 0x11 or 0x16 [O]. Phase 8 0x8BC54 sub 'U' writes 0x85E64(ch, 1), then requires the output to STAY OFF (class 0x22) → pass, 0x85E64(ch, 0) [O] — a shut-off test with the request up, at walk level ≥ 5. Phase 0x40 sets the request again after the pin-cut test and expects the output ON (the first rail-on); phase 0x80 retargets 5, expects it off, clears the request, target 0.
- **The tamper-wall premise is in doubt [I]:** the decode reads r01u's "0x9020 → latched reset loop" as confounded (r01u was already in its own ~1.5–2 s restart loop before the arm; EN90 read 00 after it). Not re-tested. **FE13 sub 16 stays un-armed regardless** — with the gate open its interlock no longer refuses, and it would stage 0x9020 at level 3–4 with none of the ladder around it; it has no stock equivalent (remove it, or make it "re-run the ladder").
- **Ladder shape [O]:** one global sequence (phase word 0x94FE2, sub-state 0x9500D), two channel slots, **only channel 0 enabled** (cal 0x1919C8 = 01) → only letter slot 0 (0x90 bit 5) can ever be set. The "channel" is a switched output whose voltage the MCU measures: 0x8C6D4 classes 0x94ED0[ch] into 0x93A92[ch] (0x22 off-with-bias, 0x66 at supply/on, …). Order: 1 (target 5) → 2 (bit 1 = 0) → 4 (output off) → 8 (request 1, must stay off, request 0) → 0x10 (off, target 3) → 0x20 (level ≤ 4 and bit 1 = 1; one-tick TIOC cut, bit 1 must drop) → 0x40 (request 1, output must come ON within tolerance 6) → 0x80 (target 5, off, request 0, target 0) → 0x100 (off) → pass mask 0xFF, sub 0xAA. Same 5 ms mode-0 list: ladder 0x44D08 → seq2 0x44D10 → pin cut 0x44D18 → J2 compose/queue 0x44D1C (a request is on the wire in the tick it is written).
- **The permanent letter comes AFTER the ladder via seq2 'U' [O dump]:** 0xAA → sequencer '.' + monitor FSM 0x23 (target 0) → level 0 + 0x6C48C(0x1838, 0xE7C7, 2) → FSM 'E' → sequencer '.' → 'L' only with FSM 'E' AND bit 1 = 1 → channel 0 class 0x33 → seq2 '3' (a diagnosis with the output off) → 'U' → letter 'c', 0x9020 for good. Driving dump: sequencer 0x9500C 'L', 0x9500D AA, pass mask 00FF, letters 'c' 0 0, 0x907D6 0x20, FSM 0x95010 'E', class 0x93A92[0] 0x66, 0x90969 00, modules 0x0F; requests 0x939B6 = 00 00 04 (slot 2 nonzero — not explained).
- **Corrections [O]:** 0x9500D = 0x40 is the supply-window ABORT, not "ladder complete" (completion is 0xAA); the ladder is its own 5 ms list entry 0x44D08 (0x91E12 is `jmp 0x92A84`, the J5 queuer); 2 slots / 1 enabled, not 3 channels; 0x93A92 is a voltage class, not a channel type; `sup_letters.c`'s header still states the inverted phase-2 test. Our code also: enable follows the request every pass (stock only in seq2 0x11/0x16 → ours would drop the letter at phase 0x80's clear); bench arm bypasses the 0x85E64 guard; no seq2 FSM, no bit-5 revoke (0x90947), no 0x90969 latch; the selftest runs after the J2 stage (one pass late); target-3 step skips phases 4/8/0x10; FSM 0x23/'E'/0x12/0x34 missing.
- **BLOCKERS before any ladder port:** **P1** the producers of 0x94ED0[0] (the output's measured voltage) and 0x94F96 (supply) — complement pairs with no movi20 store found; without them the ladder aborts at the supply window or fails phase 4. **P2** where tolerances 0x9481F/20/21 count down (counts known, not ms). **P3** monitor FSM states 0x23/'E'/0x34 and the 'E' walk rule 0x8CEDC. **P4** the wait 0x6F594 in the '3' diagnosis.
- **Bench plan:** **L1 (safe)** — full ladder + sequencer + seq2, hard-held before phase 0x40 AND phase 8's request held → wire stays 0x9000; predicts pass mask 0x003F, output class 0x22 (first check of P1), target 5 → 3, a one-tick TIOC cut drops bit 1 then C7 returns, current unchanged. **L2** — L1 + phase 8's single 0x9020 frame at level ≥ 5 (0x14 = C5): output stays off, no current change — the first 0x9020 since r01u: announce, ammeter. **L3** — the full port (needs P3/P4): 0x9020 through phases 0x40–0x80, then permanent at 'U'; brief current rise in 0x40, drop in 0x80, then the engaged rail (~1.77 A) at 'U' [I timing ~0.2–0.5 s].

**LADDER BLOCKERS P1–P4 DECODED (2026-11-12, `~/tcu_sweep/ladder_blockers_decode.md`, subagent; spot-checked this session against the dump, the countdown service and our ADC code):**
- **P1 [O]: the ladder's two inputs are AN0 external-multiplexer readings, and our firmware converts neither.** Output voltage 0x94ED0[0] (complement 0x94D48) = mux input 8 on AN0: publisher 0x89114 ← 0x90188 (10 ms moving mean, refreshed 5 ms via 0x8F62C → 0x936FC) ← 1 ms means of 8 samples (0x8F058 from the DMAC5 ISR 0x95FC4; 0x8F5A0 supervises 20 calls in 19–21 ms) ← AN0 result ring 0x885E8/0x88688 slots 2, 12, 22… (ROM sequence 0x40ECC → mux input 8), ×25000/65536 mV. Supply 0x94F96 (complement 0x94E0E) ← 0x94F86 ← plausibility 0x8D8DE (status 0x94FEC = 8 no-fault) = mux input 11, ×25000/65536; 0x907FC = min of mux input 13 ×35000/65536. Dump: 0x94ED0 = 0x326C (12 908 mV), 0x94F96 = 0x3291 (12 945 mV) — complements torn by 2–4 counts (live dump). Ours: `adc_init` scans AN1–27, never AN0; port G held all-high; nothing measures mux inputs 8/11/13. AN21 is NOT a safe supply stand-in (4.6 % below input 11; the ON test is |v − supply| ≤ 1000 mV). Spec 24 (0x96900 "SPI init") and spec 03 (0x8844C/0x885E8 "duty stream/array") are wrong: 0x8844C = the mux sequence, 0x885E8 = the AN0 result ring.
- **P2 [O]:** one countdown service 0x7C7E4 — the FIRST entry of the 5 ms list in modes 0–3 — decrements every nonzero byte in [0x94788, 0x94835) and word in [0x94734, 0x94786). 0x9481F = 3 → 15 ms (bit-1 tolerance, phases 2/0x20); **0x94820 = 0x28 → 200 ms whole-ladder timeout** (checked after every dispatch 0x8B8E4 → sub 0xF6; the stock therefore runs phases 1 → 0x100 inside 200 ms — matches the ~150–250 ms certification); 0x94821 = 6 → 30 ms (output on/off tolerance); 0x947B4 2 + 10 → 10 + 50 ms ('3' diagnosis); 0x947AC 20 → 100 ms (supply-low hold); 0x947B0 6 → 30 ms ('U' settle). 0x9414D is a retry COUNT (3), not a timer.
- **P3 [O] monitor FSM:** 0x23 — entry target 0; budget == 1 → 0x34; level 0 and 0x6C48C(0x1838, 0xE7C7, 2) == 0 → 'E' (no 0x8D020 that pass); else 0x8D020. 'E' — level ≥ 5 → 0x34; else the 0x8CEDC rule (sub 0x95011 init 0x1E: level > 0 → token ctx 0x1E; level 0 → contexts 0 → 7 → 0x19 with ~token for 0x19 — **the caller inverts: `token_arm(~tok, 0x19)`** with our verbatim W1 builder; attention 7 → resend 0x6C720, sub 0x2A; back to 0x1E after each arm). 0x12 — entry target 5, 0x8D020, no exit of its own. 0x34 — 0x8799C, back to 'E' at level 0. 0x87766(t) is called only by 0x85160: 0x23 on success, 0x12 on budget failure (sub 0xF1), 0x23 on other failures. To add to sup_walk.c: 0x87766 equivalent, cases 0x12/0x23/'E'/0x34, 0x8799C, the 0x8CEDC rule; remove the r02j `s_target3` stand-in and the dead `sup_token_context()`.
- **P4 [O]:** 0x6F594 takes no arguments and measures nothing — it returns "fault memory ready" (0x8E57D ∈ {0x55, 0xAA, 0xA5}, dump A5; or cal 0x19199B ≠ 0); its 0xA5 writer is not located. The '3' diagnosis, enable OFF: wait 10 ms, require class 0x22 within a further 50 ms, wait for fault memory ready, mask the fault bits → 0xFF (no fault) / 0xFE. '3' → 'U' also needs supply not low (< 6500 mV low, release 7500 mV, 100 ms hold), no 0x7BBB4 fault, and **0x94FCA[0] ≠ 0 — the application's output demand** (copied from model block 0x132340; dump 01).
- **0x939B8 [O]** is not a request slot: it is seq2's per-channel output status (4 = 'U' settled, 3 'U' settling, 2 state 0x11 output on, 1 class 0x22, 0 other) — the dump's 04 = channel 0 engaged.
- **L1 recipe (report §5):** platform — the AN0 mux chain byte-for-byte from 0x8F9EC (Timer G1/G2, A-DMAC ch0/ch1, ADC0 trigger mode) + 0x8F058, 0x8F62C, 0x8D8DE, 0x89114; the TIOC pin-cut switch 0x91E6C/0x91E4E. Supervisor — the countdown service as the first 5 ms entry, then the ladder (0x85678/0x85160/0x8B7A8), seq2 and the diagnosis in stock tick order. Stand-ins: 0x6F594 = 1, 0x94FCA[0] = 1, 0x7BBB4 = 1, chip-ID check = 1. L1 holds: skip phase 8's request; stop calling 0x8B7A8 at phase 0x40; in the fail path do not call 0x87766 (no untested 'E' contexts on the wire). Predicted at the hold: pass mask 0x003F, phase 0x0040, sub 0x55; class 0x93A92[0] = 0x22 with 0x94ED0[0] in 1500–4000 mV, 0x939B8[0] = 1; 0x94F96 ≈ bench supply; target 3, level 3↔4; 0x14 = C7 after the cut test (bit 1 must drop within 15 ms of a one-tick TIOC cut); wire 0x9000.
- **Still open:** the live voltages on our board — no zero-flash read exists (nothing converts AN0). Cheapest step: an **"L0-sense" build = r02j + the AN0 mux chain only (no wire change)**, or fold it into L1. Which port G bits carry the mux address (PG12–15 [I]) — a byte-faithful port avoids needing it. Timer G, previously "skip", is part of this chain.

**r02k = L0-SENSE (2026-11-12; built, NOT flashed, `build/r02k_IFla.bin`, app CRC `1DB58E96`, one block vs r02j; NOT committed at time of writing)** — the stock's AN0 external-multiplexer acquisition, so the bench can read the ladder's two inputs; NO bus-B wire change. Decode `~/tcu_sweep/adc_chain_port.md` (subagent, every write from the bytes; draft integrated and reviewed this session):
- **How the stock acquires [O bytes + manual]:** Timer G2 (12.75 µs) → A-DMAC ch0 writes the next byte of the 80-byte mux sequence (ROM 0x40ECC) to PGDR[15:8] = the mux address on PG12–15 (PG8–11 are on SSL function; PG0–7, the bus selects, untouched); Timer G1 10 µs later → AN0 conversion → A-DMAC ch1 stores ADR0 into a 160-word ring 0xFFF885E8 (two halves); Timer G4 → ADC_A scan every ~1 ms → DMAC5 → 0xFFF88130, DEI5 (vector 128, IPR 4) → 0x8F058 (1 ms means per mux input); Timer G5 → ADC_B → DMAC4, DEI4 (vector 124, IPR E). 5 ms finisher 0x8F62C/0x936FC publishes 0x90188 (mux 8, output node = 0x94ED0[0]), 0x907E2 (mux 11, supply = 0x94F86/0x94F96), 0x907E4/0x907FC (mux 13 mean/min), 0x9018A (mux 10), 0x907EE (mux 14). The 0x6BD6E publisher is a plain value/complement store, so these ARE the ladder inputs. **Spec 24 has DEI4/DEI5 swapped** (DMAC5 = ADC_A, DMAC4 = ADC_B).
- **Integration:** new `platform/sh7254r/plat_adcmux.c` (app only; empty bodies under TCU_RAM_APP; the loader never links it): 0x96010 after the 0x8FB96 block, 0x97744 + 0x8F9EC after the Timer C init and before 0x91ED4 (stock order); Timer G starts with the existing ATUENR |= 0x3FF. Vectors index 120/124 → v_dei4/v_dei5 (were default_handler — would have halted on the first DMA end). New 5 ms task at phase 4 (0x8F62C + 0x8F5A0 status ramp). Linker NOLOAD reservations at the stock addresses: 0xFFF88130 (+0x3C), 0xFFF884BA (+0x22), 0xFFF885E8 (+0x142), 0xFFF90000 (+0xB0), 0xFFF90184 (+8), 0xFFF90628 (+4), 0xFFF910FC (+0xC). **Deviation D1:** the mux sequence lives at 0xFFF88500, not the stock's 0xFFF8844C, which overlaps our .sup_stage2 walk staging. D5: our continuous ADC scan is stopped and waited out before 0x967F4. Not ported: the rest of 0x8F058, fault 561, profiling hooks, 0x89114/0x8D8DE (left for L1). Undocumented writes ported verbatim: 0xFFFFE8E0/ECE0 = 0xAA550001, byte 0xFFFFE825 = 0, ADCER bit 14. IPR20 stays 0 (plat_intc_setup). First float use (task level only).
- Host 22/22, transcript unchanged; vectors and sections verified in the ELF; no existing cell inside the new ranges. Install `/tmp/install_r02k.py` (sigs `/tmp/r02k_sigs.json`), witness `/tmp/witness_r02k.py` (adds mux 8/10/11/13/14, min13, the 20-call span 0x910FC, the ms counter 0x90628, ADMATCR1 0xFFFE6064 read twice, AN21 raw).
- **PREDICTION (rule 1), r02k:** [high] wire and gate exactly as r02j — 0x14 C7, gate 1, target 3, level 3↔4, J2 TX 0x9000, every job completing, no restarts, current unchanged. [high] the chain runs: span20 ≈ 20 000 µs (not the preset 20000 frozen — the ms counter climbs), ADMATCR1 changes between reads. [I strong] **mux 8 (output node, output never requested) = 1500–4000 mV** (the stock passes ladder phase 4 on this bench every boot); mux 11 (supply) ≈ 0.998 × bench supply (≈ 1.047 × our AN21); mux 13 ≈ supply, min13 ≤ mux13 by ≤ ~400 mV; mux 10 ≈ 24.9 V; mux 14 ≈ 1.02 × supply. **Refutations:** span frozen / ms counter flat → DEI5 not delivered; ADMATCR1 constant → A-DMAC not running (check our RSPI ISR's ADMATE0 writes first [I]); all mux outputs equal → the address is not reaching PG12–15; mux 11 ≈ supply but mux 8 outside 1500–4000 → the off-with-bias model fails here (L1 would fail phase 4); **mux 8 ≈ supply → stop, check the ammeter**; any change in 0x14/gate/level/jobs → "no wire change" refuted.

**r02k BENCH RESULT (2026-11-12 13:07 install, block 0x40000 sig `61d0d2f858d89b80`, all verifies OK; power-cycle; witness 13:12–13:20 `/tmp/witness_r02k.log`, `/tmp/witness_r02k_dump.log`) — PREDICTION REFUTED: r02k RESTART-LOOPS (~50 ms cycle, app life ~7.3 ms); THE ACQUISITION WORKS** [O]:
- **The chain works and the inputs are as predicted:** mux 8 (output node, never requested) = **2939 mV** (predicted 1500–4000 ✓ — the off-with-bias class 0x22 L1 needs); mux 11 (supply) = 13 361 mV; mux 13 = 13 366; mux 10 = 24 920 (predicted ~24.9 V ✓); mux 14 = 13 566; all stable across reads; the DEI5 ISR runs (~5 calls per boot by the 0x90628 counter). Read 0 for min13 / span / ADMATCR1 is an artefact of reading mid-loop (most reads land in the 41 ms boot-side window).
- **The loop:** `s_rst.boots` 323 → 9128 (~20/s); TCNTA@entry 0xA1AD every boot (41.4 ms of boot side); `now_ms` 6–7; WTSR 0, wovf flat; **no 0x7F5 exception frames** in 6 s of sniffing (118 heartbeats, one per boot) → not an exception, not the MCU WDT; last breadcrumb = superloop (01) at entry + 7.33 ms. Nothing in our code resets on ADC/wake.
- **Opening record:** boot 1 is byte-identical to r02j's through 7.31 ms (first batch, J1 8E04, W2 8406 → fb F0, the sel-0 token 88FF at 5.55 ms, the second batch J3/J4/J2/F/J6); the record cap (16) ends there. The next event — the second J1 completion (~7.37 ms; in r02j this J1 carries the ask, 8E05) — never comes: **death in a ~40 µs window around the second J1 burst, every boot** → [I] an external reset by the supervisor. Boots ≥ 2: J4 (SSLB2) replies 0D8B/0E37 instead of FF03, an AB-class W2 reply, and in the current boot replies doubled/shifted a slot (J3 AA45 AA45, J6 …AADF AADF…) — a disturbed chip/bus state after the first death.
- **Open — what r02k changed in the supervisor's view.** Candidates (one variable each): (1) the A-DMAC ch0 → PGDR[15:8] stepping (PG12–15 toggling every 12.75 µs; port G live: PGCR2 00AA, PGCR1 AAAA, PGIOR FFFF — PG8–11 on SSL function, as the stock); (2) DEI4 (IPR E, above RSPI-B 4 and the walk 6) / DEI5 (IPR 4) ISR latency on the CPU-fed RSPI bursts — shifted replies point here [I]; (3) the 5 ms finisher (first float code); (4) Timer G outputs (TCRG 0x12, CMPOEG). The stock drives bus B by DMA (no CPU per frame) — ours is CPU-fed, so ISR latency is a deviation the stock never had.
- **Next (decode before drive):** cheapest discriminator first — e.g. r02k with DEI4/DEI5 interrupts left disabled (DMAs still run; publish from the 5 ms task by reading the completed ring half) → tests (2) alone. Extend OPEN_N or add a death-window breadcrumb so the next read shows the last burst. The bench can go back to r02j (known-good, gate open) at any time.

**r02l — THE DEFERRED-START DISCRIMINATOR (2026-11-12; built, NOT flashed, `build/r02l_IFla.bin`, app CRC `6C6610F2`, one block vs r02k; NOT committed at time of writing):** r02k with ONE variable changed — Timer G (TGE, ATUENR bit 7: the only clock of the whole ADC/mux chain — mux stepping, both scans, both DMA interrupts) is held at init (ATUENR |= 0x037F) and set by `plat_adcmux_start()` from the 5 ms ADC task once `sched_now_ms() ≥ 500`. Everything else of the chain is configured at init exactly as r02k. Instrumentation (no wire effect): OPEN_N 16 → 32, and the record keeps rx[4] (J6's 0x14 frame) in the former pad word. **.noinit moved 4 bytes:** `s_rst` 0xFFF9E62C (boots +4 = 0xE630), `s_wovf_count` 0xE648, `s_open_n` 0xE64C, `s_open` 0xE650 (slot stride 512). Witness `/tmp/witness_r02l.py`, install `/tmp/install_r02l.py` (sigs `/tmp/r02l_sigs.json`). Host 22/22.
- **Reasoning note:** with bus B CPU-fed, ISR latency only stretches inter-frame gaps (the next frame starts only when we write it — no overrun possible), so "DEI latency" is weaker than first stated; the doubled/shifted replies of boots ≥ 2 may be after-effects of the first death.
- **PREDICTION (rule 1), r02l — two outcomes, each decisive:** (A) **death moves to ≈ 500 ms** (app life ≈ 0.5 s, boots ≈ 2/s) → the chain itself provokes the reset regardless of the conversation; next split: mux stepping (A-DMAC ch0/G2) vs scans/DEI vs finisher vs Timer G outputs. (B) **no death** — the run looks like r02j (C7, level 3↔4, J2 9000) AND the mux readings appear after 500 ms (mux 8 ≈ 2.9 V) → the chain is only incompatible with the opening (the ask/gate window); the fix is ordering, and L1 can proceed with the stock's chain started after the gate. I lean (B) [I ~55 %] — nothing in the chain is known to reach the supervisor, and the r02k death sits exactly at the opening's second J1 burst. A third outcome (death still at ~7 ms) would mean the chain's init (not its running) is the cause — the TGE hold is then refuted as the variable.

## The rules (each one earned)

These are the user's standing directives. They are not suggestions.

1. **No flash without a prediction.** Before staging any bench flash, derive and state whether it will succeed from evidence already in hand. If you can derive failure, do the desk work instead. Bench flashes are the scarce resource. (Stated 2026-11-11 after I staged a flash whose failure was derivable in two sentences.)
2. **Decode before drive.** A flash executes a decoded recipe, never a guess. Ghidra/disassembly/session-log evidence first.
3. **Inventory logged data before new runs.** Before proposing a bench run for information, assemble the answer from existing captures/dumps/decodes. The user challenges redundant runs — rightly.
4. **"Stock-shaped" ≠ decode.** Verifying that words/parameters match the stock is not verifying behavior. Sequence, timing, mechanism, and count must each be verified against the stock's actual bytes. Two session-losing errors (the silence window, the pool order) were "words right, structure unverified."
5. **User bench truth over theory.** The user's physical observations are ground truth. Label inferences as inferences. Correct records immediately when refuted.
6. **Bench protocol:** the wake storm is armed ONLY against a dark bus (arming against a live board = instant false catch); storm and the install chain run as SEPARATE background tasks; one process per serial port (the meas/witness/ammeter tools conflict); `meas 0x02` (and verify the reply) before every read session — the periodic 0x398 stream races the meas replies and garbles reads; the ammeter never co-runs with the storm/install; announce any current-moving event before it happens; power cycles are the user's physical act — off, then arm, then on.
7. **Preserve displayed messages and written flash.** Never rewrite history the user has seen; diagnostics go in prose and the status file.
8. **Subagents for deep decodes; you validate before flashing.** The pattern that works: brief an agent with the full evidence chain and corpus pointers, validate its commit (build, tests, CRC — run them yourself), then apply. The user will tell you when they want an agent vs. direct work — "we've already logged and identified this; implement it now" means do it yourself, now.

## Where everything lives

- `docs/spec/` — the specification, specs 1–26. Specs 19–26 are the function catalog and top-down syntheses (chronology, the 14-job supervisor protocol, walk reconciliation, latch conditions, chip ID). **Spec 21 §1 is the power-on chronology — the boot truth table.**
- `firmware/` — the open reimplementation. Platform (`platform/sh7254r/`), supervisor service (`bsw/sup/`), the app (`app/main.c`). Host build + tests: `build/host` (21/21 must pass; the transcript tool is a test). Target build: `build/sh2a`.
- `firmware/tests/unit/transcript.c` — **the byte-identity verification tool**: drives 100 passes, prints every bus-B word. Any sequence drift fails the build. Extend it; it is the project's most valuable new instrument.
- `~/tcu_sweep/` — the decode corpus: `first500ms_diff.md` (the stock's opening, word by word), `boot_jump_law.md`, `gate_timeline_mining.md`, `walktmr_map_fix.md`, `duty_wedge_trace.md`, `w1_wedge_fix.md`, plus the 47-unit Ghidra sweep. Read `first500ms_diff.md` first.
- Bench tools: `~/tcu_bench_tools/` (watch.py passive heartbeat, amps.py ammeter → /tmp/amps.log, chatter_watch.py). Install chains: `/tmp/install_*.py` (regenerate per flash — see the sig-table law below). Measurement reads: `tcu_bench.meas(b,1,[0x2A,0,addr...])` via `tools/bench/tcu_bench.py`.
- The stock reference images: `~/Downloads/260924131233 1034420267_IFla.bin` (pristine September read-out — the restore source). Our images: `build/*_IFla.bin`.

## The bench mechanics (established laws, don't relearn them)

- **Install:** `tools/build_image.py --app build/sh2a/tcu_app.hex --base <previous>_IFla.bin -o build/<new>_IFla.bin --stock-ident --gap-keep-above 0x50000`. The sig table for the install script = `/tmp/r01h_sigs.json` (the learned table) with `0x40000/0x10000` set to `None` (force-write). **The skip check means "live == TARGET sig"** — feeding it current-flash sigs writes nothing (cost one wasted cycle). After each install the chain learns the new block sig back into the table.
- **The flash cycle:** user says "off" → arm storm + launch install (two background tasks) → user says "on" → storm catches → install writes (~4 min) → user "off"/"on" again → witness reads.
- **Cell addresses:** nm `build/sh2a/tcu_app.elf` every time — never trust remembered addresses (two wrong-struct-read incidents). The cell map is `firmware/bsw/sup/sup_cells.h`, pinned at stock RAM addresses.
- **The walk timer is a DOWN COUNTER** (DCNTD00 @0xFFFFF5B0, vector 224 = underflow): load `0xB000` (~4.5–4.95 ms at its ~9–10 MHz clock), then strobe DSTRD0 @0xF585. Never a compare register — that misread cost a day.
- **The tamper wall:** forcing the letters arm past the chip's selftest gate triggers a latched anti-tamper reset loop (~1.15 s period, until power-off). The interlock in `sup_letters_bench_arm` refuses while the gate is closed — never bypass it.
- **The stock reference measurement:** genuine stock → rail at **<0.5 s** from power-on; by the first readable moment (+13 s): `0x14=C7`, gate=1, letters='c' earned, EN90=0x20. The chip is healthy; the bench is rail-capable; the stock earns certification in its first ~150–250 ms.

## The immediate work (open, in priority order)

1. **Decode and close the five enumerated transcript deltas** (from the tool's output, f716fd7): (a) the FLT re-fire count/position (`0x14&0xE0` law on our faulted D5 chip — is a second FLT per pass stock-true, and where?); (b) the walk words' interleave position (ours queues inside the batch; the stock's rides the tick context); (c) J7's slot; (d) the ask rides ~2–3 passes late (ours P25, stock ~P22 — the one-shot latency chain); (e) the duty token bytes (ours FF/0F/F0/00 vs the stock dump's 01/F1/41/B1 — verify the token law: same selector → same token?).
2. ~~Fix the FSM-advance wedge~~ — **closed, no wedge** (see the CORRECTION above: r02a was restarting). **Replaced by:** port the post-certification batch split (10 ms for batch/reply-proc/F, J2 staying at 5 ms) keyed on ladder completion, and decode the `0xFFF9414D` countdown that would take a gate-closed unit to mode 1 anyway — see THE STOCK'S TASK RATES above.
3. **Then the flash, with the stated prediction:** r02d. Prediction: no restarts ([I] — r01y's batch-from-boot and r01v's immediate-arm openings both ran without a restart loop); `8406` and the ask inside the first ~15 ms; the FSM leaves supervise at ~3.6 s and DUTY climbs (host trace); **the gate: no prediction** — first bench test of the pool order and of the stock's monitor cadence together. Witness with the heartbeat cells first (strict init/armed alternation every 100 ms = restarting).
4. Deferred: solenoid setpoints at engagement (spec 12/E13 — an interactive decision with the user), the 0x398 read-race root cause, DCSELDn0 field decode, D7 J1_ERR map.

## How to work here

- The user is at the bench and hands-on: they power-cycle on request, watch the ammeter, and know the hardware's sounds and smells. They ask sharp methodological questions — "why are we trial-and-erroring?", "why is the order reversed?" — and the right response is to own the drift and fix the method, not to defend it. They will say "have a subagent X" when they want an agent and "implement it now" when they don't.
- Commits carry full evidence in their messages (see git log — they read like lab notes). Follow that style.
- When refuted, bank it immediately: update the project-state record, state what the refutation eliminates, and name the next discriminator. Clean refutations are progress here — seven of them narrowed the gate hunt from "everything" to five enumerated deltas.
- The percentages in README.md's status table move with milestones — update at each one.

## The people

A human/AI pair produced this project (see CONTRIBUTORS.md). Early commits were Claude-assisted; the recent arc is ZCode/GLM with human direction; you are the next collaborator. The work product discipline matters more than the tool: everything cited, everything reproducible, nothing claimed beyond its evidence grade ([M]/[O]/[I]/[D] — see README's conventions).

With Timer G held, the chain's init does NOT kill the opening → **outcome (C) refuted; the chain's RUNNING killed r02k's opening** [O].

Every job still completes each pass (USE equal), REQ/GRANT mostly 01/01, J2 TX 9000 throughout, fsm 08, target 03.

- **Re-reading r02k [I, strong]:** in both builds the last breadcrumb is the SUPERLOOP at a fixed µs, then nothing — no completion, no further superloop pass, no exception frame. That is a CPU that stops running at a deterministic moment (a stuck ISR or an interrupt storm at a level above the superloop), then is reset externally about 41 ms of boot side later. It is not "the supervisor kills a live conversation at the ask". The ask is cleared of blame by r02l's opening.
- **Prime suspects (desk; one variable each):** (1) DEI4 — IPR E, the highest level in the system; the handler only clears the DMAC4 flag; ADC_B + DMAC4 complete "every 2 scans"; a stuck-flag or re-trigger storm at level 14 would freeze the superloop with no exception. (2) ADCSR1 = 0x10 (ADIE) — if ADC_B's ADI is not the DMA trigger on our setup, an unrouted ADI vector. (3) The 1 ms DEI5 body (`adcmux_1ms`) at IPR 4 — it shares the level with RSPI-B, which fits the W2 errors in survivors. **Next:** put a breadcrumb at entry and exit of DEI4/DEI5 (codes 07/08) plus a per-vector counter in .noinit, so the next death names the ISR it died in. Optionally hold DMAC4/ADC_B alone (ADC_B feeds nothing the ladder needs: mux 8/11 come from AN0 + ADC_A/DMAC5).

**r02m = r02l + DEATH INSTRUMENTATION ONLY (2026-11-12; built, NOT flashed, `build/r02m_IFla.bin`, app CRC `FBD57412`, one block vs r02l)** — no functional change; host 22/22; the loader is unchanged (the new breadcrumb in the shared exception body is app-only):
- DEI4/DEI5 wrappers write breadcrumbs at entry (07/08) and exit (87/88). The default exception/unwired-vector body writes `0xDE000000 | PC[23:0]` before its 0x7F5 announce and hang.
- `.noinit` per-boot ISR entry counts `s_isr_n[4]` (DEI4, DEI5, RSPI any, walk) at 0xFFF9E634. The previous boot's final counts are copied to `s_isr_prev[4]` at 0xFFF9E624 in `plat_wdt_early_capture`.
- **.noinit moved:** `s_rst` 0xFFF9E64C (boots 0xE650), `s_wovf_count` 0xE668, `s_open_n` 0xE66C, `s_open` 0xE670. Witness `/tmp/witness_r02m.py` (prints both ISR count sets), install `/tmp/install_r02m.py` (sigs `/tmp/r02m_sigs.json`).

  - prev code **07** (DEI4 entry, no exit) → stuck inside the DEI4 handler;
  - **87/07** with `s_isr_prev[0]` far above ~25 → a DEI4 storm;
  - **08/88** with a huge `s_isr_prev[1]` → a DEI5 storm;
  - **0xDExxxxxx** → an exception or unwired vector at that PC;
  - still **01** with ordinary prev counts → the CPU stops in the superloop itself (a busy-wait, or the bus/flash stalled under DMA), not in an ISR.

  The timing (one instrumentation write per ISR) may shift the death by µs. It should not change the outcome.

**r02n = r02m + THE ROLLING TRACE RING (2026-11-12; built, NOT flashed, `build/r02n_IFla.bin`, app CRC `45A295DE`, one block vs r02m)** — diagnostic only; host 22/22:

  - if the last event is a `C` or `M` with nothing in flight, the reset is not a reaction to a bus-B frame. The candidates then turn to the chain's own output (the PG mux stepping or Timer G) at that phase.

  The trace adds ~2 µs per event under a full mask, which may move the death again without changing its cause.

- **What this leaves [I]:** stepping PG12–15 every 12.75 µs corrupts bus B — AB-class replies, J4 `0Dxx`, bad W2s — until the supervisor resets the MCU. The stock steps the same pins every boot without harm, so the difference is in OUR port G / bus-B setup.
- **Next (desk, rule 2):**
  1. which pins carry bus B on our build (SCK/MOSI/MISO/SSLB0–3 — the SSL pins on PG8–11 or elsewhere) and whether any CPU code writes PGDR or the PG direction/PFC registers while the DMA writes it;
  2. whether ch0's destination is PGDR's high byte (a byte write) or the whole word — a word write would also rewrite PG0–7/PG8–11 data;
  3. the stock's PFC/PGIOR for PG8–15 against ours (0x962E2 routes pins AFTER the timer config; ours routes first).

**DESK DECODE — WHY THE MUX STEPPING CORRUPTS BUS B (2026-11-12, after r02o) — mechanism NOT found from the desk; what is ruled out [O], and a discriminator:**
- **The DMA write is byte-wide to PGDR[15:8] [O manual §12.3.2(1), Fig. 12.3]** — ch0's destination is fixed in hardware, and the sequence data must sit in the upper 4 bits of each byte. PG0–7 (PGCR1 = AAAA: SSLA0–5, SSLB0, SSLB1) are never written. The lower nibble writes 0 to PGDR bits 11:8, but PG8–11 (PGCR2 = 00AA: SSLB2, SSLC0–2) are on function, so the data does not reach the pins [O manual Table 23.8].
- **Port G set-up equals the stock's [O]:** stock 0x961F0 writes PGCR1/PGCR2/C844 = 0, PGIOR = PGDR = FFFF, PGIR = PGDSR = 0 — our port_init writes the same; 0x962E2 adds AAAA/00AA as ours does. The live r02o read (C830..43 = FFFF FFFF 0000 0000 0000 0000 FFFF 0000 00AA AAAA) matches. No runtime code of ours writes any port G register (grep), and the stock has none either (0x9A148 only checks).
- **RSPI-B register set equals the stock's** (baseline_review_transport: SPCMD AF11/AF01/AF20 field-for-field, SPSCR = selects−1, SPCKD/SSLND/SPPCR 0, SPBR 5). The one known delta is SPND = 1 for every job, where the stock uses 0 for J1/J5/J6/J4 (N1) — one extra RSPCK of next-access delay, not a corruption mechanism [I].
- **ADMATE0 is cleared:** it holds the TE flags of channels 56–63 only (manual Table 12.5); ch0/ch1 are not in it. Our ISR's F3/FC writes cannot touch the mux channels.
- **The alias window:** our bus-B staging (0xFFF88400–0x8847F, J7 0x885E0) does not overlap the mux sequence (0x88500–0x8854F) or the ring (0x885E8–0x88729) [O nm].
- **What remains [I]:** the stock steps the same pins with the same register set, and is fine. The difference left on our side is that **bus B is CPU-fed while the stock's is A-DMAC-fed** — the timing relation between a PG12–15 edge and a bus-B frame differs. Alternatively, PG12–15 switching couples into the supervisor's lines independently of our bursts; then the stock would be exposed too, which argues against it.
- **Next discriminator (one variable), r02p:** keep the stepping, but SUSPEND it during every bus-B burst — clear CMEG2 at the bus-B launch, set it again at burst end, so no PG12–15 edge occurs while SSLB is asserted. (A) clean like r02o → the edge-during-burst overlap is the mechanism, and r02p is also a working acquisition for L1: the ring entries taken during a burst keep the previous address [I: a few % of samples]. (B) still corrupts → the stepping disturbs the chip regardless of bursts; next, scope PG12–15 against SSLB/RSPCKB on the bench, or compare the stock's ch0 rate during its bursts.

The lab abort path releases too. The writes run under SR.I = 15 and are guarded until `adma_init` has armed the chain. Not stock behaviour — a discriminator. Host 22/22.

- **Next options:**

  2. a correct acquisition with the same no-edge-in-burst property — hold BOTH ch0 and ch1 (or stop Timer G1/G2 together), so the slot mapping holds — before any L1;
  3. explain the walk polled-fallback rise (hold/release under SR.I = 15 inside the walk ISR's launch path is the first suspect).

Ours went 0x23 → 0x34 → 'E': in state 0x23 the walk budget (0x8D1BA() == 1) ran out before level 0 and the 0x1838 era were reached, and 0x8799C then brought it to 'E' at level 0 (its "previous 0x23" branch: 0x93B93 = 0x34, 0x93B99 = 1, stage 0x1838). **DESK DECODE — THE 0x23 BUDGET OVERRUN (2026-11-13, after r02t) — the law is ported 1:1; the overrun is the chip not stepping down once per arm; the cause cannot be decided from logged data** [O law, I cause]:
- **The law [O, decompiles 0x8D136/0x8D1BA/0x8D020 re-read against sup_walk.c — equivalent line for line]:**
  - 0x8D136(0), called at 0x23 entry (entry flag), sets the budget 0x9417D = `*(u8*)0x191A1B (= 3) + L + 2` with L = the reported level at that moment (0 → budget 0).
  - 0x8D020 then decrements it once per pass in which the target is not reached, and adds it back if the arm was BUSY (walk not idle: 0x6C802/0x6C720 returned nonzero). **Net: one budget unit per successful arm** (token or resend), none per blocked pass.
  - State 0x23 reads 0x8D1BA() first each pass; == 1 → the 0x34 path. **So the budget allows L + 4 successful arms to bring the reported level from L to 0.** In the stock, budget 1 also sets the lamp bit 0x94F84 |= 8 (0x8D020) and the 0x34 path 0x94F84 |= 0x800 — **an overrun is a stock FAULT indication** (lamp bits not ported, D2). The stock driving dump's 0x93B93 = 0x23 says the stock's descent fits its budget.
- **Ours took ≥ L + 4 arms without reaching level 0 [O by the law].** The host model steps one level per token and finishes in budget. On the bench the steady states already show the chip moving the level up between arms (3↔4 at target 3, 5↔6 at target 5, 0↔1↔2 in 'E'), so a descent is net −1 only when an arm lands before the chip's own rise.
- **Candidates (one variable each) [I]:**
  1. **The walk polled fallback** — present since r02q's burst hold (~14–28/s; r02o ~0.1/s; the stock has none). A polled walk stamps late (r02f law), so a token can land outside its slot: the chip does not step, or answers attention 7 → resend, which costs budget.
  2. **The 5 W2 error completions** in r02t's boot (frames = 0; r02r and r02s had 0). They are new with the descent/'E' walk. A failed W2 reply → the reply processor's error path → resend.
  3. A higher L at 0x23 entry than modelled (phase 0x80 drove the level toward 5) — this only scales the budget, so on its own it cannot overrun it.
- **No logged data decides it** (rule 3): the trace ring holds the last 32 events (seconds after), the opening record ends at ~7 ms, and the descent is at app ~0.58–0.65 s.
- **Discriminator (no wire change), r02u:** r02t + a `.noinit` **descent recorder**, armed by the 0x87766(0x23) call and stopped at 'E' or 0x34. One entry per monitor pass: TCNTA, budget, reported/expected level, attention, arm kind (token ctx / resend), arm result (busy or armed), the walk event path (ISR or poll) since the last entry, and the W2 error count. Read once after boot. It names which arms failed to step the level, and whether they were polled or errored. Then fix the cause, flash, and confirm 0x93B93 = 0x23, before L3b.

**THE MODE-1 SWITCH, PORTED (2026-11-13; subagent, `~/tcu_sweep/mode1_port.md`; validated this session: sh2a builds, host 29/29, the default image's mode cells inert)** [O decode from the bytes]:
- **Task objects:** the 5 ms task is two objects (0x41378 mode 0, 0x4134C modes 1–4) sharing countdown 0x9355A; the 10 ms task is 0x413A4/0x413D0 sharing 0x9353C, so the phase survives the switch. The 10 ms task fires with the first 5 ms pass and every second one after. `0x66E06` applies a pending mode at the END of the 10 ms task in mode 0 (the 10 ms tail hook 0x67A42).
- **Condition chain:**
  1. 'U' (pass U).
  2. Seq2 resolves at U+1: module 7 = 0x0F.
  3. The background module manager 0x7DAA4 sets module 1 = 0x0F, then boot state 0 → 1, and the boot-state-1 list's last entry 0x7DB70 sets module 2 = 0x0F, then state 2.
  4. `0x6F0B4` sets pending 0x93B34 = 1.
  5. The switch lands at the end of the first 10 ms task: 0x848CF = 0x93B33 = 1, state 3.

  'U' → switch takes 2–3 passes (dump stamps: state 1 at 170.6 ms, mode-1 init at 180.6 ms). The mode-1 init list touches nothing on bus B, the walk or the ladder.
- **What moves in mode 1:** reply proc 0x8EE52, the pin cut, the F stage and the batch (J3/J4 condense; J6/J1/J5; the bus-A ID kick) go to 10 ms. The countdown, 0x9181C, the publishers, 0x6F0B4, seq2 and the J2 stage stay at 5 ms, and the sequencer becomes 0x85160 direct. The 2.5 ms monitor is unchanged. **Mode-1 bus B:** J2 every 5 ms; J3 J4 F J6 J1 J5 F2 every 10 ms.
- **CORRECTIONS to THE STOCK'S TASK RATES block:**
  - module 2's 0x0F comes only from 0x7DB70 in the boot-state-1 list;
  - module 7 is set one pass AFTER 'U', **and on every ladder failure path** (seq2 0x22 retry exhaustion, 0x16 supply abort) — "0x9414D" is a retry count, and a failed certification also goes to mode 1;
  - 0x66E06 applies at the end of the 10 ms task, not every pass;
  - the pin cut and 0x7CBA8/0x7CC1E/0x7CB6E/0x7AB48/0x7AB9C also go to 10 ms;
  - list positions there are 0-based;
  - mode 0 has no 20/30/50/100 ms tasks.
- **New hold H5 `SUP_MODE_HOLD`** (defaults to H4): a failure path could reach mode 1 even with H4 held, so H5 keeps the default image in mode 0. It records the refused mode in `s_held` (0xFFF84C00). `SUP_L3B_ON_TARGET` is removed (the gap is ported); L3b with H5 held is refused. Host: `test_ladder_l3b` 'U' F117 → module 7 F118 → pending F119 → switch → first mode-1 pass F120, with every J2 after 'U' = 0x9020 and no discards. New `transcript_mode1` / `_28us` judge 349 mode-1 passes. Cells: 0x93B32/33/34 state/mode/pending, 0x848CF, 0x93B7E/81 modules 1/2, 0x9353C/0x9355A countdowns, `s_sw_frt` 0xFFF84C04.

**SOLENOID OUTPUT CHAIN — STOCK DECODE (2026-11-13; read-only subagent, `~/tcu_sweep/solenoid_chain_decode.md`; the bus-A layout claim verified in our code this session)** [O unless marked]:
- **CORRECTION to "Do NOT port 0x97198 (Timer E) blindly — 100 % duty on the solenoid pins":** the 8 lead-frame solenoids are driven by the **4 SPI driver chips on bus A**, not by Timer E. Timer E drives:
  - group A: 1 kHz on/off outputs (TOE31 = park hold, TOE30/32 unknown);
  - group B: 50 µs pulses at 1 kHz, one per driver chip (TOE42/43/50/51/21, likely a sync to the chips' 0x8E = 0x9F setting [I]);
  - TOE52: internal 10 kHz;
  - 11 pins with no writer: TOE00–03, 10–13, 23, 40, 41, 53. They sit at DTR = CYLR = 0xFFFF, so they go high ~33 ms after TEE every boot; function unknown.

  0x97198 itself sets only TCRE/PSCRE/TIERE/SSTRE/TSTRE/IPR (no CYLR/DTR/TOCRE). The real hazard is ORDER: the stock clears TEE (0x96F68(700)) before 0x97198, zeroes the used channels' duty with TEE off (0x9136E group A, 0x919A0 TOE52, 0x90010 → 0x8FF66 group B), and turns TEE on only in the mode-0 init list (0x91F3E). Ours has no TEE-off window. The "stock inverts TOE31" comment in `plat_toe_out_init` is wrong (0x97214 sets TONE only for polarity 0; group A is polarity 1).
- **Solenoid current is gated on the supervisor (0x83DB0, 5 ms):** the request passes only when seq2[0] = 'U' (bytes 0x83E22) and the rail node 0x94ED0 ≥ 7400 mV; requests < 500 (0.1 mA) are zeroed. The chain is 0x89540 (letters '!'/'C'/'2') → 0x901A0 (per-chip records 0xFFF91108 + 0x1C·c, setpoint = (I + 0x9090A)/6, every F-stage pass) → cyclic 0x924B0 + config 0x92344. **Current regulation is inside the chips; no MCU current loop.** There is no separate chip enable: mode letters, the switched rail and the 0x83DB0 gates are the enables. Before 'U' the stock sends mode 0x15, setpoints 0 and the config job every pass.
- **Rail-off full-on tests [O code, I purpose]:** ladder phase 0x10 (0x94114) and the '3' diagnosis (0x94115) command mode 4 (full on) on solenoid ch 0–7 and duty 1000 on TOE30/31/32 while the rail is OFF. Our ladder port does not do this yet.
- **The 1.77 A [O dump]:** requests (0.1 mA) A 500, D/B/E/C 8500 each, WK 500, Sys 500, park release 0; all Timer E outputs 0; dither 0. That fits 1.77 A at ~5.5–7 Ω coils [D] (spec 12's "~25 Ω" conflicts).
- **OUR bus A UP TO r02u [O code, verified]:** `drv_cyclic_build` was chip-major, but the engine sends frame i to SSLA[i mod 5]. So chips 0 and 3 got mode **0x1A (current control)**, chip 2 got 0x12, and the setpoint rows went out as 0x0000 reads. **Five channels (A, D, C, Sys, park release) sat in current control at the chips' unknown power-on setpoint**, live whenever the rail was on (the ≤ 30 ms ladder windows of r02t/r02u showed no ammeter step). The config job was never sent.
- **User decisions (2026-11-13):**
  - D5: stage 0a as its own flash before L3b.
  - D2: the bench has the **real mechatronic**.
  - D3: reproduce the stock's constant-high level on the 11 TOE pins when Timer E is ported.
  - D4: port the rail-off full-on tests with L3b.
  - D1 (which solenoids, setpoints, order) is still open.

**r02w = L3b: THE PERMANENT LETTER, MODE 1, AND BUS A UNDER THE ZERO-REQUEST LETTER LAW (2026-11-13; built, NOT flashed, `build/r02w_IFla.bin`, app CRC `AA351E4E`, one block vs r02v; NOT committed at time of writing)** — host 29/29:
- **Supervisor:** H4 (and H5 with it) released on SH (`sup_ladder.h`). After the ladder's 0xAA and FSM 'E': sequencer 'L' → channel 0 class 0x33 → seq2 '3' (the output-off diagnosis) → 'U' → letter 'c', 0x907D6 = 0x20, **J2/F carry 0x9020 permanently**. Module 7 follows one pass later, then the mode-1 switch (`test_ladder_l3b`: 'U' F117, switch at the end of F119, first mode-1 pass F120). Mode-1 bus B: J2 every 5 ms; J3 J4 F J6 J1 J5 F2 every 10 ms.
- **Bus A — the zero-request letter law [O 0x83DB0 decompile read this session; I where noted]:** all application requests are 0 (not ported).
  - In seq2 ∈ {0x11, 0x16, '3'}: mode 4 (FULL ON, letter 'C', mode byte 0x1F, setpoints 0x7FF [I: 0x9090A taken as 0]) when 0x94115 & 1 ('3' diagnosis), or 0x94114 & 1 (ladder phase 0x10) with seq2 0x11/0x16 — **D4, the stock's rail-off full-on tests**. Otherwise mode 0 → '2' → 0x8815.
  - In any other seq2 state ('U' included): mode 1 → letter 0 → **0x8810** on chips 0–3; chip 4 (empty) stays '2' / 0x8815.
  - The config job's 0x88 row follows.
- **Two safety measures of ours (deviations), found while porting:**
  1. **Same-tick refresh:** our cyclic runs at 10 ms, so a full-on frame staged just before 'U' could have held the chips full-on for up to ~10 ms with the rail closed. `drv_letter_refresh()` (weak hook) now runs right after seq2 in the 5 ms supervisor tick. It sends a new cyclic the moment the law's mode bytes change (retrying next tick if bus A is busy), before the J2 stage composes that tick's word. The stock gets this from its own same-tick ordering. Witness `s_drv_refresh_n`.
  2. **Interlock:** full-on is staged only while the staged J2 word is 0x9000 and both request cells are 0. Host-tested: the phase-0x10 and '3' states → 881F / EFFF / F7FF; with J2 0x9020 or a request up → 8815; 'U' → 8810.

**r02w BENCH RESULT (2026-11-13 14:24 install, block 0x40000 sig `18c7498e127e4a1b`; boots 14:25 and 14:39 under the ammeter, CAN log `/tmp/rawcan_r02w.log`) — CRASH LOOP: AN IMAGE-BUILD DEFECT, NOT THE L3b CODE** [O]:
- **Symptoms:**
  - no measurement reply, no heartbeat; CAN carries only 0x7F5 exception frames `DEAD 001F EDFA 0000` (faulting PC 0x001FEDFA, SR bits 15:0 = 00xx), repeating every ~113 ms — a crash → WDT reset → crash loop;
  - current a flat ~168 mA from the first 0.2 s (r02v: ~183 mA) — the app crashed at once on every boot;
  - no current step, no load driven.
- **Cause [O]:** r02w's `.text` grew to **0x50284** (66 332 bytes of text from 0x40870), past the 64 KB block at 0x40000. `tools/build_image.py --gap-keep-above 0x50000` UNCONDITIONALLY replaced every byte from 0x50000 with the stock reference, and only block 0x40000 was installed. So our const tail at 0x500E4–0x50284 ran from stock bytes. That tail held `s_tp_cfg`, **`s_tasks` (the scheduler's task table, 0x500FC)**, `s_ipr_map`, `s_uds_app`, `s_sensors_cfg` and `__clz_tab`. The scheduler took a stock calibration-pointer word (0x001FEDFA at 0x50100, a pointer table into cal) as a task function and executed calibration data. r02v and earlier fit below 0x50000 (r02v ran).
- **Fix (tool):** `build_image.py` now never overwrites application bytes. It keeps reference bytes only from max(`--gap-keep-above`, app end rounded to 0x100) and prints a loud line naming the block the install must now write. Rebuilt r02w (same app, same ELF): **app CRC `FE3535E0`**, image blocks 0x40000 **and 0x50000** differ from r02v. Every header/vector/.text byte matches the ELF except the header CRC (0x40044) and the stock-ident pointer (0x40075). `s_tasks` now reads 0x0004DAA4…
- **Install:** `/tmp/r02w_sigs.json` sets 0x40000 AND 0x50000 to None (force-write). Block 0x50000 otherwise holds stock bytes, unused by our app, and is rewritten as such.
- **Lesson:** a growing app must be checked against the image layout on every build: block list vs previous, app end address. **The r02w prediction stands as written** — the L3b code never ran.

Remaining before D1 (first solenoid current): bus-A transport integrity (decode the error mechanism; port the stock's DMA-driven bus A or fix the CPU-fed path), then the zero-request law → request law (0x83DB0 gates, 0x89540 letters, 0x901A0 setpoints), then Timer E stage 0b.

**BUS-A ERROR DECODE (2026-11-13; read-only subagent, `~/tcu_sweep/busA_errors_decode.md`; the code and manual claims validated this session)**:
- **The arithmetic [O/D]:** RSPCK 1.667 MHz (SPBR 5), ~10.8 µs a frame (~12–13 µs CPU-fed); cyclic 45 frames ≈ 0.55–0.6 ms, config 40 ≈ 0.5 ms; ~8 500 bus-A RX interrupts/s. **OVRF cannot come from latency alone**: the CPU starts frame k+1 only after harvesting frame k, so every OVRF retry proves two frames were in flight at once.
- **Lead mechanism [I strong; the code and manual verified this session]:** the feed clears SPSR (`*sr = 0`) and returns to `rte` with no read-back of the flag. **Manual §8.10.1: "Perform dummy read the interrupt source flag after clearing it to ensure that the interrupt request that should have been cleared is not received again erroneously."** The ISR body does not check SPRF on entry. A re-entry harvests a stale word and feeds the next frame while one is still shifting. That gives OVRF (retries), a late OVRF latched where SPRF never sets again (the err = 0 stall the reaper ends), or a silent slip (duplicate rx words — visible in r02x's CYC/CFG rx dumps, previously read as "torn reads").
- **Trigger [I]:** P-bus contention from the A-DMAC mux chain delays the posted SPSR write. r02w's bus A died at +0.623 s, 123 ms after the chain starts; the r02q G1/G2 hold covers bus-B bursts only, which is why bus B is nearly clean. **The r02k–r02n bus-B "doubled replies" (AA45 AA45, AADF AADF) are the same signature** [I ~55 %] — the r02q hold may have been treating a symptom.
- **Ruled out:** A-DMAC masking (ADMATCR56–59 = 0, TE cannot set); registration races (launch, chained config launch and reaper all under SR.I = 15); other RSPI-A writers (`plat_drv_xfer*` unreferenced). Minor: the drv handlers validate nothing; an OVRF inside the body was cleared without setting `err`; the reaper did not snapshot SPSR.
- **The stock [O bytes]:** launcher 0x96468 writes ADMATCR56/57 (frame count), ADMAAR56/57, ADMATE0 = 0xFC, SPCR = 0xF8. A-DMAC ch57 feeds TX on transmit-empty and ch56 drains RX — two frames in flight in hardware, no CPU per frame. Only ch56's transfer-end interrupt (vector 445, IPR 4); burst-end ISR 0x96590 / 0x965E4 with one re-dispatch, then an error. Porting it is the stock-true end state, but multi-variable (all bus-A buffers in the 0xFFF88000 alias window; the stock cyclic slot 0xFFF88518 collides with our relocated mux sequence at 0xFFF88500).

The r02q hold treated a symptom and is retired (compiled out, `MUX_HOLD_ENABLED 0`). Our acquisition now runs exactly as the stock's.
- **Status:** certification, the engaged rail, mode 1, a clean bus A carrying the stock's post-'U' driver content, and stock-exact ADC acquisition — all under open firmware. **Next toward D1 (first solenoid current):**
  1. the request law (0x83DB0 gates incl. the 'U' and ≥ 7400 mV rail checks, 0x89540 letters, 0x901A0 setpoints = (I + 0x9090A)/6, the 0x85ED4 engaged-output monitor already ported) with all requests 0 — a no-current flash;
  2. Timer E stage 0b (TEE off → 0x97198 → duty zeroing → TEE on, the 11 pins high per D3);
  3. D1: the user's choice of first channel and setpoint.

**THE STOCK SOLENOID REQUEST LAW, PORTED (2026-11-13; subagent, `~/tcu_sweep/request_law_port.md`; validated this session: sh2a + host 35/35, the SH request guard read in the source, FPU context saved in every ISR wrapper)** [O decode from the bytes]:
- **What is ported:**
  - `drv_reqlaw.c`: **0x83DB0** → `drv_rq_law()` — chip enable 0x8FD94 / cal 0x1918C1 = 0x1F, channel mask 0x00FF, the 'U' gate, clamp 0x2FFA, fault class (stand-in no-fault), the full-on tests (0x94115 / 0x94114 with masks), the rail-undervoltage law (flag 0x93978, release 7900 mV), the min-current law, outputs 0x94ED4 / F58 / FD8 / FA0 / 0x9397A / C;
  - **0x89540** → `rq_letters()`;
  - **0x901A0 / 0x90118** → `drv_rq_compose()` — the per-chip records 0xFFF91108 + 0x1C·c, setpoint (I + 0x9090A) × 0x3E2AAAAB truncated, dither, config bytes; the high-current rule and the 0x238 head check (counted);
  - **0x924B0 / 0x92344** → `drv_stage_from_records()`. r02w's `drv_letter_code` is removed.
- **CORRECTIONS:**
  - 0x9090A is not a current correction: it is a rounding offset cycling 0 → 4 → 1 → 4 … each 0x901A0 pass (bytes 0x902A8, initial cal 0x1918BB = 0). The current correction is **0x837E0 → 0x9337C** (adaptive; dump 0 4 4 2 6 2 2).
  - The rail-undervoltage law zeroes only requests ≤ 4000 (400 mA) below 7400 mV (the "→ full on" branch needs cal 0x190510 bit 1, never set).
- **Dump reproduction [O host]:** with the dump's inputs, every law cell matches (modes, active flags, letters, currents, records +7 = 1A 1A 1A 12 15), all 40 config words, and **44 of 45 cyclic words**. Word 12 is ED89 vs the dump's ED8A; a channel-4 correction of 7 (cell holds 6) reproduces all 45, so the dump's staging was composed one correction step before its records [I].
- **Safety frame:** on SH the request is a compile-time 0 (0xFFF94EE8 never read). `CELL_RQ_REQ` is pointer-to-const, the setter/injector are `_Static_assert(0)`, `-DDRV_RQ_REQUEST_SOURCE=1` is an `#error` — five ctest checks compile with sh-elf-gcc and must fail. The same-tick refresh now runs the law. The full-on interlock applies twice (law and compose; mutation-tested). FE10 still cannot drive anything.
- **Stand-ins / not ported:** fault inputs no-fault; correction 0x837E0 = 0; dither 0; the diagnostics (0x8422E, 0x89F5C, 0x897AC, 0x8A788, 0x84644, 0x84392, 0x8493A); the 0x226/0x238 reports; the reduced-frequency / group-B tail; config bytes constant; the chip-ID harvest 0x921E0 (0x9091B stays 0 — **read `rx_drv_id` on the bench before porting it: ID 0xBD with a second byte < 0x10 would set mode bit 5**). Cadence kept at 10 ms + the same-tick refresh (stock F stage: 5 ms mode 0, 10 ms mode 1) — a stated deviation.

**TIMER E STAGE 0b, PORTED (2026-11-13; subagent, `~/tcu_sweep/timerE_0b_port.md` with a write-for-write table against the stock; validated this session: sh2a + host 40/40, loader hex byte-identical to HEAD, group-A constants and the 0x40CB4 table read from the stock bytes)** [O]:
- **The stock's order, app init only (`stock_gpio_init`):**
  1. TEE off (ATUENR bit 5 only — the stock's 0x96F68(700) clears 0x2BC; ours keeps Timer C/D/G running);
  2. `te_97198` byte-faithful after the Timer C block;
  3. TEE off again after the Timer G init (0x91FA4's tail);
  4. `te_919a0` (TOE52, cycle 200, duty 0, not routed);
  5. `te_9136e` — group A TOE30/31/32 (+E22/E33): cycle 2000, **duty 0**, TCNT offsets 327/1452/702/1077/702, TONE 0, TIERE 0 (the cycle-match ISR 0x6A680 not ported);
  6. `te_90010` / 0x8FF66 — group B TOE42/43/50/51/21: cycle 2000, duty 100 (50 µs), stagger 1960/1560/760/0xFE48/0xF808;
  7. TEE back on at the existing `ATUENR |= 0x037F` (the 0x91F3E position).

  **12 writer-less pins** (the decode said 11; its own list names 12) keep reset DTR = CYLR = 0xFFFF and go high 32.8 ms after TEE-on, as the stock (D3). `plat_toe_out_init` now runs in the loader only.
- **Safety:** `TE_GROUP_A_DUTY = 0` under `_Static_assert`. On SH `plat_toe_out_set` exists only with `TCU_M4B_OUTPUTS`, and `TCU_M4B_OUTPUTS` without `TCU_RAM_APP` is an `#error` — five sh-elf-gcc guard checks. A full-disassembly scan shows that only `_plat_app_init` and `_te_chan` touch Timer E addresses in the flashed app.
- **FINDING [O manual Table 13.7 + stock bytes]:** regs.h had `TOCRE3` 0xFFFFF8C2 (really TIERE3) and `RLDCRE3` 0xFFFFF8C6 (a reserved byte). **The old `plat_toe_out_init` never enabled the reload, so `plat_toe_out_set` never changed TOE31 on any build** — every past park-hold test via FE11/park.c (RAM app) drove nothing. **With the fix, the RAM app's park hold is LIVE** — treat FE11/park.c as live before that build is ever run. The loader keeps the old code (byte-identical) and still writes the reserved byte.
- Pin routing before timer config (ours) vs after (stock 0x962E2) does not matter: TOE pins hold their initial low level until the counters run, and TEE is off during config.
- **Not ported:** the TIERE arm on TOE30/31, the RAM shadows, the runtime writers (0x91500, 0x912C6/0x91318, 0x8FC5E, 0x919D6), 0x90010's Timer F PWM-input set-up and Timer D compare triggers, 0x96F68's other enable bits.

**M4b — PARK OUTPUTS: DECODE + USER DECISIONS (2026-11-13/14; `~/tcu_sweep/park_outputs_decode.md`, read-only subagent; cal claims re-checked this session: pull-in reload 0x1919C3 = 4, modulator cal 0x1918E7 = 0, hold curve 0x19157C = 5400 mV @ −30 °C … 9380 mV @ 140 °C):**
- **Park release (bus-A ch 7):** a steady request of **10000 (1.0 A, current-regulated, letter '!')** whenever the park target is not P (0xE8888 → 0x7B6D8 slot 7); chip 3 8812 → 881A, F0 slot 3 → F682/F683. No pulse. Not inputs: brake, engine, line pressure, the park sensor. Chain: park target 0xFFF8D6D6 → … 0xCFFDE → park request 0xFFF8AE29 → 0xE88FA (release on iff request = 0; hold on iff the lock decision = 0).
- **Group A:** **TOE31 = park hold** — needs 'U'; 20 ms at 100 % pull-in, then duty = curve 0x19157C(temperature 0x94F9C) / rail-node mV × 1000 (≈ 515 ‰ at 25.7 °C / 13.0 V). TOE30 is a battery-compensated coil (function unknown), forced off by 0x94FEA = 1. TOE32 is battery-supplied (≥ 6.7 V), function unknown. **No cycle-match ISR needed.**
- **Order:** leaving P, release and hold come on in the same tick, open loop. Entering P, release drops at once and hold drops on the lock decision. The park "state" 0xFFF8BC70 is a timer model (release 150 ms, engage ≈ 200 ms at 27 °C), not the sensor.
- **The stock has Auto-P** (the dump: no lever source → stationary → target 8, both park outputs off). The park position sensor's channel is not resolved (lead: 0x8DF2E inputs AN5/AN6, DTC group 98).
- **The M4a audit:**
  - `range.c` ≈ stock mode-4, but it treats an invalid output speed as moving (it would release park on a sensorless bench);
  - `park_ctl.c`'s sequencing is invented and must be replaced;
  - M4a's drive values are wrong (release full-on 2047 vs stock 1.0 A; hold 100 % vs pull-in + compensated).
- **USER DECISIONS 2026-11-14:**
  - **D-M4-1** dry bench (no pump, no driveline);
  - **D-M4-2** park hold first;
  - **D-M4-3** port the stock producer of 0x94F9C first;
  - **D-M4-4/5** port the TOE30/TOE32 laws AND the rail-off group-A full-on tests.

- **Staging:** `DRV_GA_STAGE` (CMake cache, `tcu_app.elf` only).
  - **0 = every demand 0, full-on tests held (default)**;
  - 1 = TOE31 demand 10000, tests held;
  - 2 = tests released, demands 0, still behind the bus-A rail-quiet interlock.

  Static asserts: only slot 11 may be nonzero, and only 0/10000 per stage. No SH demand setter (`#error`). `plat_te_ga_*` (the only Timer E group-A writers) refused outside `drv_groupa.c`, absent from the loader. A runtime stage guard forces a disallowed DRLD to 0 (counted) and clamps every DRLD to the cycle. 11 sh-elf-gcc compile checks; mutation-tested.
- **Stand-ins:** 0x94FEA = 1 (TOE30 forced off, as in the dump); fault class no-fault (0x938F6 not ported); fault path 0x65 inactive; coding byte 0. The RAM app no longer carries this law (its 64 KB limit).

**D-M4-6 / D-M4-8 (user, 2026-11-14):** retire `park_ctl.c` (replace it with the stock logic), keep `park_mon.c` as the cross-check; lever = Chrysler type-1 CAN frames from the bench tool; output speed = the stock's own rule.

**THE STOCK RANGE/PARK LOGIC, PORTED (2026-11-14; subagent, `~/tcu_sweep/range_logic_port.md`; validated this session: sh2a + host 58/58, the two driver call sites pass a park demand only when it equals the compile-time bound 0/10000):**
- **What is ported:**
  - **Arbiter** `app/range.c` = 0xA548C / 0xA557C, the special rows 0xA54A4 (the decompile inverts one speed test; the bytes need D650 ≥ 320), all five modes, mode 4 = 0xA57DA; result 0xFFF8E549 / 0xFFF8D6D6.
  - **Output law** `app/park_law.c`:
    - park request AE29 = (target 8 && no aux) || (aux && no car-wash inhibit);
    - lock = inhibit 0x89C30 clear && (aux || (AE29 && (locked || interlock 0xE8B56)));
    - release on iff AE29 = 0, hold on iff lock = 0;
    - timer model 0xE89DE (release 150 ms; engage from map 0x1B5044, 200 ms at 27 °C);
    - demands 0xE8888 = 10000 / 0.
  - Cells pinned at the stock addresses (`.park_*`).
  - `park_ctl.c` deleted. `park_mon.c` keeps an independent arbiter recompute vs 0xFFF8D6D6; a mismatch over 2 cycles or a corrupt copy latches both demands 0 until power-off (ours).
- **THE SPEED RULE [O]:** the stock arbiter has **no validity term** — output speed > 80 rpm counts as moving, and moving without lever P → target 6 → **park released**. On this bench our speed code reports 0 rpm with good quality when no edges arrive, so the logic sits in Auto-P. **Do not turn the output shaft without lever P.**
- **OPEN [I]:** the stock lever object 0xFFF8304C has no writer in flash except a safety reset; the dump holds 8 there, contradicting the arbiter's own reason 0x17. Our arbiter takes `profile_fca.c`'s type-1 CAN evaluation (5–8, 0 if no source is valid). Stood-in inputs (dump values): 0x89C30, 0x89CF2, 0x8E69B, 0x89C13 (← our 0x94103), 0x89D00, 0x89CB8, 0x89D49, 0xFFF8B272, D650, D648/D652.
- **`DRV_GA_STAGE` NEW MEANING:**
  - 0 = safe (both park outputs 0, tests held; header default, RAM app);
  - **1 (CMake default) = the park outputs FOLLOW THE PORTED LOGIC** (release ∈ {0, 10000} under the 30 s limit, hold ∈ {0, 10000}), tests held;
  - 2 = tests released, both park outputs 0.

  Demands leave `app/park.c` only through `park_slot7_release()` / `park_slot11_hold()` (`bsw/drv/drv_park.h`), each with exactly one caller in the SH image. There is no setter and no UDS path.
- **The 30 s limit (D-M4-7) now:** it accumulates release-on time across the power cycle (per law pass while the release is requested and seq2 is 'U') and at 30 000 ms latches the release off until power-off. Cell `s_rq_rel_win` 0xFFF95B7C: state, t_last, acc_ms.
- **Bench tool:** `park` / `park-check` defaults fixed (`--bus 1`, `build/sh2a/tcu_app.elf`). LeverSender: 0x2EA on bus 1 (CRC-8 + counter), 0x210 on bus 2, every 10 ms (firmware timeout 50 ms). **Caution:**
  - the tool uses BOTH Teensy ports (0x210 on ACM1 = the ammeter's port), so the ammeter logger cannot run during a lever test — watch the meter by eye;
  - a USB stall > 50 ms gives a one-tick Auto-P flicker;
  - `park-check --hw` at settle 2.0 spends ~14 s in R/N/D, so two runs exhaust the 30 s budget.
