**PARK POSITION SENSOR — DECODE + ZERO-FLASH READ (2026-11-14; decode `~/tcu_sweep/park_sensor_decode.md`, read-only subagent; zero-flash read on the running r03k this session, script `scratchpad/parksens_read.py`):**
- **Decode [O]:**
  - **Inputs:** switch evaluator 0x8DF2E inputs 2/3 = **AN5 / AN6** (ADC_A scan, already in our AN table 0x9018C[5]/[6]).
  - **Reference:** NOT the battery but **AN0 mux input 15** (ring slots 16/36/56/76 → 0x900B0 → 0x9360C, ×15000/65536 → 0x907E8 → latched 0x93942 by 0x89320) — the shared sensor supply [I].
  - **Conversion:** ratio = AN × 10000 / supply → 0x934C4[k]; bands (cal 0x1913A8) 100 / 1900 / 5000 / 8000 / 9500; state = ratio < 5000 (active low). Bands 1 and 3 are valid, the rest are electrical faults.
  - **The safety pair object 0xFFF8EA28** (0x141B42/0x141BA2) → position 0xFFF8EA40: A0B1 → 0, A1B0 → 1, both active → 2, fault / both inactive → 3 (qualifier 0xFFF8EA42 = 2).
  - **DTC group 98** (0x141E06): P07B2 / P07B8 = contact A / B electrical; P0850 m0 both inactive, m1 both active > 100 ticks. **The stock dump's group-98 status 0x3EBB = failed** (like the bench-absent groups), so the stock flags P0850 on this bench.
  - **Into the park module:** D61A[0x2E] = position, [0x38] = qualifier, D5F8 = (position == 1 && qualifier == 0) → arbiter 0xA57DA (stationary + D5F8 → target 8 even in R/N/D), 0xA54A4 rows 2/3 need D5F8 = 0, P1B14. The park law / timer model never reads the sensor. **On this bench D5F8 = 0, so the sensor affects neither target nor outputs**; our D648 = 3 / D652 = 2 stand-ins are the live stock values.
  - **OPEN:** which contact means "Park" — P1B14 implies position 1 = engaged, but then the arbiter could never release P from standstill. Decode 0xA4F0C / 0xA4FC4 / 0xC58AA / 0xC60CE before wiring the position into range.c.
  - **CORRECTIONS:**
    - spec 14 §5's 0x141A2C works on 0xFFF8E788 (not 0xFFF8EA38), and 0xFFF8EA40 comes from 0x141BA2;
    - D61A[0x2E] is a position code, not a park request;
    - spec 04's "supply reference" is mux 15;
    - `sensors.c` divides by AN21 (≈ 50.65 % here — on the band 2/3 edge).
- **ZERO-FLASH READ on r03k [O]:**
  - at rest (Auto-P), with lever R (park release + hold ON: modes ch 7 = 03, TOE31 DTR 0x0436), and back in P: **AN5 = 6265–6267, AN6 = 6265–6267; mux-15 supply = 8980 ± 3 mV; ratio = 6976–6979 (69.8 %) on both contacts — band 3, "both inactive", exactly the stock dump's 69.8 %**;
  - **no change at all when the park outputs switch** (decode [I ~85 %] ✓ — dry bench, no piston motion; the ratiometric supply cancels any sag);
  - the park meas went 08 17 → 06 16 (R) → 08 15 (P) as designed;
  - ≈ 4.5 s more of the release budget used this power cycle (accumulated ≈ 18.7 s).
- **Verdict:** the sensor is present and electrically healthy, in the "both inactive" band — the stock reads the same and flags P0850 m0. On a dry mechatronic out of the gearbox its target is absent [I]. A read-only port flash would add no information beyond these reads. **Next options:**
  - the user's optional physical check (a magnet at the sensor during a zero-flash read) to identify which input is "Park";
  - the 0xA4F0C / 0xC58AA decode to resolve the contradiction;
  - or move on to M5.

**TIF12 IS THE OUTPUT SPEED SENSOR (2026-11-16, user: the sensor the steel test triggered sits at the rear of the transmission)** [O user + bench]:
- **Speed channel 0 = TIF12 (PF6) = output shaft.** In spec 02 §4.1's routing, **stock application signal B (RAM 0xFFF93048, ← channel 0) is output speed.** Signal A (0xFFF93044, ← channel 1 = TIF13) is then the turbine speed [I, the only other routed channel; not yet bench-confirmed].
- **OUR CONFIG IS SWAPPED:** `app/main.c` `s_sensors_cfg = { turbine_ch 0, output_ch 1, … }` feeds TIF13 into `output_speed` and so into the park arbiter's D646 (`app/park.c` `output_speed_d646`). The consequences on r03k:
  - a turning output shaft is NOT seen by the arbiter (it stays Auto-P);
  - a turning TIF13 shaft above 80 rpm WOULD read as "moving", and without lever P that releases park (stock rule 0xA57DA).
  
  Harmless on a dry bench with no shaft turning. **It must be fixed (turbine_ch 1, output_ch 0) before any shaft turns or any vehicle test.**
- **FIXED IN SOURCE (2026-11-16, user: "follow the stock firmware's lead"):** `app/main.c` `s_sensors_cfg = { turbine_ch 1, output_ch 0, … }`, and spec 02 §4.1 updated with the bench resolution. Not flashed: r03k on the bench still has the swapped assignment until the next build is installed.

- **The user's lead:** AN6 is the turbine speed sensor. A magnet on the turbine grabs at evenly spaced points, a tone wheel [I].
- **The test:** steel was swept across the second sensor's face in two rounds, the second close in and fast. Nothing changed: no AN channel moved > 300 mV, 0x90958 stayed 00, and F12/F13/F14 stayed 9/3/0.
- **Evidence against AN6 = turbine speed:**
  - [O decode] the stock reads AN6 as park contact B (0x8DF2E input 3 → safety pair 0x141B42 → DTC P07B8 "park position sensor B");
  - [O bench 2026-11-14] AN6 switched (mostly together with AN5) only under the magnet at the park Hall sensor;
  - a speed sensor is a pulse input (Timer F), as the output sensor on F12 showed.
- [I] Turbine speed is most likely TIF13, whose input is live (3 edges at power-up). This sensor did not switch on steel at its face. Candidates: it needs the wheel's tooth gap and speed, it is not connected in this setup, or it is not the sensor that was tested.

**PARK POSITION + OUTPUT SPEED DECODE (2026-11-16, read-only subagent, `~/tcu_sweep/park_position_speed_decode.md`; the key claims re-checked this session against the flash bytes) — EVERY STOCK CONSUMER READS POSITION 1 (CONTACT A / AN5 ALONE) AS PARK ENGAGED; THE BENCH'S "MAGNET = OUT OF PARK" CONFLICTS, AND THE CONFLICT IS OPEN** [O bytes, I where marked]:
- **Re-checked here [O]:**
  - Position mapping 0x141C7A–0x141CAC: status 0 → qualifier 0; A (+0x0C) = 0 and B (+0x10) = 1 → 0; A = 1 and B = 0 → 1; else 2.
  - **P1B13** 0xC5E12–0xC5E22: fails on model D61A[0x06] == 4 AND position ≠ 1.
  - **P1B14** 0xC5E8A–0xC5E98: fails on position == 1 AND model == 1.
  - Arbiter mode 4 0xA57E6–0xA5846: lever ≠ 8, D646 ≤ [cal] and 0xA7716() (D5F8) == 1 → target 8 / reason 0x15; else lever 7/6/5 → 6 / 0x16; else D646 ≤ [cal] → 8 / 0x17.
  - Cal 0x1918DF = 01 (signal A ← channel 1), 0x1918E0 = 00 (signal B ← channel 0); safety ppr 0x195DCD/CE = 0x28/0x1E.
- **Model encoding [O subagent, 0xE89DE; consistent with r03k's park-check]:** 4 = engaged (the dump's P state), 1 = released (150 ms after the request drops). So P1B13 = "model says engaged but the sensor does not say 1" and P1B14 = "model says released but the sensor says 1": **the stock expects position 1 in Park**. The other consumers agree [O subagent]: 0xA4FC4 (P confirmed only with D5F8 = 1), 0xA4F0C / 0xA8336 (flag when the target is P and D5F8 = 0), 0xC5912 (group 192, faults on 1 → 0/2 transitions while P is commanded). Arbiter mode 2 (not selected in this calibration) treats position 0 as not-P.
- **The conflict:** if "magnet at the sensor = out of Park" (the user's 2026-11-16 inspection) is right, the stock would set P1B14 while driving, P1B13 while parked, and force P at every stop in D. A stationary P → D release in mode 4 needs D5F8 = 0. No other release path is decoded: the pre-mode rows need D650 > 320 plus lever-object fields with no decoded writer. Candidates: (a) the mechanical reading is the other way round (the magnet sits on contact A in Park); (b) an undecoded writer of the lever object 0xFFF8304C. **Discriminator:** move the real park mechanism with the valve body fitted and read AN5/AN6/0x90958. **Until then the sensor stays read-only and out of `range.c`.**
- **Group 98:** positions 0 and 1 are equally valid. P0850 m0 = both inactive; m1 = both active > 100 ticks; P07B2/P07B8 = contact A/B electrical.
- **Output speed path [O subagent]:** TIF12 → 0x7C3AA → 0x93048 → slot 0x4C790 → 0xFFF8E7A0+0x10 → 0x143686 rpm → 0xFFF8E7C0 → 0x14C23E selection → **0xFFF8EE6E = D646**; turbine TIF13 → E7A0+0x48 → 0x14C818 → 0xFFF8EF06. **This confirms today's channel fix (output = channel 0) from the bytes.**
  - 0x93044/0x93048 are time words, not speeds (dump FFFFFFFF).
  - On a sensor fault, D646 takes a substitute 0xFFF8E798 (321) × 0xFFF955B4 / 10000 with quality 1 ([I] wheel speed × axle ratio 3.21). On a supply fault the value is held (quality 2). With no pulses it decays to 0 (forced 0 below 5 rpm). The arbiter never reads the quality.
  - **The safety layer uses 40 pulses/rev for output and 30 for turbine** (cal 0x195DCD = 0x28, 0x195DCE = 0x1E [O]); our speed path uses Timer F's 60. If these are pulses per revolution [I], **our output rpm reads 2/3 of the stock's D646**, so our 80 rpm "moving" threshold trips at 1.5× the stock's shaft speed. Resolve before any shaft turns.
- **CORRECTIONS (subagent, not yet applied to specs):**
  - spec 13: 0xC5E02 is P1B13 (model 4 && position ≠ 1), not a transition timer; the transition logic is 0xC5912 (group 192); D61A[0x2E]'s producer is 0x141BA2;
  - park_sensor_decode §2.4: candidate (a) "position 1 is abnormal" is refuted;
  - spec 02/07: signals A/B are time words, not speeds.

**REFERENCE: Land Rover 8HP70 workshop overview (2026-11-16, `docs/reference/8hp70-landrover-l320-overview.md`)** [M, first-gen 8HP70 / Land Rover TCM — a different variant]:
- **Coil resistances at 20 °C: EPRS 5.05 Ω, MV1 10–11 Ω, MV2 (park hold) 25 Ω.** These match our supply-step estimates: release ≈ 11 Ω, hold ≈ 20 Ω, pressure ≈ 7 Ω warm. **Spec 12's "~25 Ω" is the park-hold coil**, which resolves the r03e conflict.
- **Park lock:** a torsion spring engages park; main pressure on the piston releases it; MV2's claws latch the piston out of park while energised. This matches the ported stock law.
- **Speed sensors:** turbine and output are Hall sensors inside the mechatronic.
- **No park position sensor in this variant**, so it cannot settle our AN5/AN6 polarity question.
- **EPRS polarity:** C and SYS fall with current, A/B/D/E/WK rise. This **conflicts with spec 12 §1.1 (FCA) on D and E** — open, not applied.
- Also: ratios, clutch-B standstill decoupling, 32 bar relief.

**REFERENCE: ATRA 8HP45/70 seminar (2026-11-16, `docs/reference/8hp45-70-atra-seminar.md`, VW/Audi part codes)** [M]:
- **G182 input speed sensor reads a MAGNETIC RING** (alternating poles on the drum next to the P2 planet). That is why steel never triggered the turbine-side sensor, and why the user's magnet grabs the turbine-side drum at even spacing.
- **G195 output speed sensor reads the parking-gear teeth** (steel), which matches TIF12.
- **G747 "Parking Lock Sender" is the park position sensor**, which is AN5/AN6.
- **Next zero-flash test:** magnet with alternating poles across G182, combined watch on F13 (the 15:27 magnet test watched only AN channels).

**PARK SENSOR: LATCHING AND THE PARK-SOLENOID MOVE (2026-11-16 17:36–17:46, zero-flash watches on r03m, `/tmp/pksens_watch{,2,3,4}.log`)** [O; I where marked]:

  - [I strong] **The two Hall elements are latching switches:** one pole sets, the opposite pole resets, and the state holds in between. Which face of the magnet was used at each touch is the user's to confirm.
  - Consequence [I]: the real target must present a pole to each element as the park selector moves; a magnet that merely comes and goes would leave the state stuck.

That matches the stock's own "both inactive / P0850" reading on this bench.
- **Outputs unchanged throughout** (target, D6D6, request-law modes).
- **Still open:** which element is mechanically "Park". It needs the valve body on the transmission and the selector moved.

**PARK TARGET ON THE SELECTOR LEVER (2026-11-16, user photos + inspection)** [O user; I where marked]:
- The target is a **magnet block with a curved face** on an arm of the park selector (detent) lever in the transmission case. It sweeps an arc around the selector shaft. Its face shows witness marks (a worn band down the middle, rubbing on two edges), so it rides at or near contact with the sensor housing.
- **The user's reading:**
  - lever OPEN (not Park), as in the photos: the block **covers** the Hall sensor;
  - lever CLOSED (Park): the block is **not covering, or only partially covering** it.
- **Reconciling with the stock code, which expects position 1 = A (AN5) alone in Park and position 0 = B (AN6) alone out of Park [I]:**
  - The stock treats both inactive (nothing covered) and both active as faults, so in each valid position exactly one element must be active.
  - **Park, partial cover:** the block's edge over element A only gives A alone = position 1 = Park, consistent with the stock.
  - **Not Park, full cover:** the elements appear to latch (17:36–17:42 today). If the block carries two poles along its arc, full cover can set B and reset A, giving B alone = position 0 = Not Park.
  - Under that reading, the user's mechanical description and the stock's mapping agree. Both "magnet at the sensor = out of Park" and "position 1 = Park" can hold.
- **To check by hand (no power needed):**
  1. Probe the block's face end to end with the hand magnet for a pole change (attract → repel).
  2. With the valve body fitted, which element the block's edge sits over in Park.
- **Still decided only on the assembled transmission.**

**CORRECTION — THE PARK HALL ELEMENTS DO NOT LATCH (2026-11-16 18:01–18:02, zero-flash watch on r03m with a second magnet, `/tmp/pksens_watch5.log`)** [O]:
- **The test:** a second magnet, placed and then pulled away each time, about 20 touches.
- **Every element released on its own** when the magnet was removed, within ~0.1–0.5 s, back to both inactive (position 3, P0850 m0).
  - AN5 alone → position 1; AN6 alone → position 0; both → position 2, then P0850 m1 after 1.0 s, exactly as on r03m's first magnet test.
  - Sweeps pass through A → both → B (or the reverse), so **the two elements sit side by side** and one magnet covers one, then both, then the other.
- **The "latching" inference above is withdrawn.** The earlier minutes-long both-active holds (17:36–17:42 today and 2026-11-14) came from something else: [I] the first magnet left close enough, or a steel part near the sensor magnetised by it.
- **The user believes the lever's block is a single-pole magnet.** With side-by-side elements and a single-pole block, a valid reading needs the block over **one element only** in each end position. Full cover of both would be position 2 → a P0850 m1 fault.
  - So in Park the block's edge must sit over one element, and out of Park it must have moved on to sit over the other.
  - The stock code says the Park element is A (AN5) [I from code]. Which element the block reaches first from the Park end is what the assembled test must show.
- Outputs unchanged throughout.
- **The sensing face (user photo, 2026-11-16):** the sensor is one sealed housing with a grey rectangular face (pin on top), which the lever's block sweeps across.
- **Face-mapping attempt (18:05–18:08, `/tmp/pksens_watch6.log`):** the second magnet on the left/right/top/bottom halves of the face, then held off the face, **always switched both elements together** (position 2 → P0850 m1). No placement isolated one element. So the halves cannot be mapped by hand with the magnets on the bench; the elements are too close together for them.
- **Which element is Park stays open** until the real lever is tested on the assembled transmission. Single-element readings so far came only from partial placements beside the sensor (2026-11-14, and r03m's first magnet test).

**M5 DECODE — THE REQUEST PRODUCER AND THE STOCK SHIFT MATRIX (2026-11-16, read-only subagent, `~/tcu_sweep/m5_request_producer_decode.md`; key claims re-checked this session)** [O unless marked]:
- **The producer:** 0xDC4C4 is the only caller of the setter 0xE53DA(req, p, mode, prio, source). It sends:
  - Sys (source 0x15), WK (source 0x16), the shift-phase sources (ids 1–0x11);
  - then a **holder** loop: every element A–E that no other source set that cycle gets **0 or 20000 mbar, mode 1 (on/off), priority 4, source 0**, chosen by 0xDC1E2 from **table 0x505A4** (pool 0xDC410 = 0x000505A4, re-checked).
  - Mode 1 values strictly between 0 and 20000 raise fault 0x19F (0xE4E5C).
- **Current law in mode 1:** the curve and temperature correction are skipped (0x107CFC). The current is **8500** for an applied normally-closed or a released normally-open element, else **500** (cal 0x1FEF6C/6E = 500 / 8500, re-checked). Partly re-checked: 0x107D00 branches on pressure == 0 → constant by type byte; nonzero → threshold compare (0xBAA58, 12750).
- **Table 0x505A4** (2 blocks × 10 rows R, N, 1–8 × 6 bytes; byte 0 unused, then A B C D E; re-checked):
  - **block 1 = the full shift matrix:** 1 = A B C, 2 = A B E, 3 = B C E, 4 = B D E, 5 = B C D, 6 = C D E, 7 = A C D, 8 = A D E, R = A B D, N = none (the known ZF 8HP matrix);
  - **block 0** (power-flow state 0x89C30 = 0, no power flow) = one brake: R/1/2/7/8 → A, 3/4/5 → B, N/6 → none.
- **The dump's P set reproduced:**
  - inputs (re-checked): 0x89C2B = 03, 0x89C30 = 00, so block 0 / gear 3 → B only;
  - Sys forced to 20000 (reason 0x01) and WK 0; request 0x8B828 = 4E20 with +7..+9 = 01 00 04;
  - result A 500, D/B/E/C 8500, WK 500, Sys 500 — the r03f constants.
- **Correction:** 0xFFF89C30 is the **power-flow state** (0 none / 1 forward / 2 reverse; writers 0xCFE56/0xCFDDE), not a park inhibit. The park law's stand-in 0 remains correct for the bench (P).
- **Garage shifts [O structure; timings/thresholds open]:**
  - lever range → direction request AE28 (D → 1, N/P → 0, R → 2; table 0x63445);
  - power-flow state from table 0x6354C, so R↔D passes through 0;
  - gated by engine running (state 3 of 0xFFF8E6A8; engine speed > 430 rpm, cal 0x1FEE94/96);
  - the engagement machine 0xE28E overrides the holder (states 1–6 all off, 8–0x11 A only);
  - the neutral-idle machine 0xE298 releases the slip element (B in 1st).
- **Limp:** no separate substitute-gear table found. **With the rail off, C, D and E are normally open and pressurised → 6th gear at maximum line pressure [D]**: the hydraulic default gear.
- **Inputs:** CAN already receives engine speed, torques, pedal and brake. Missing: the stock's input conditioning (0xB912C/0xB986C), the engine-state machine 0xFFF8E6A8, the line-pressure strategy (Sys in drive), and WK in drive.
- **Proposed stages** (each 850 mA coil ≈ +0.38 A, r03e; on the dry bench these verify currents and the bus-A words, not hydraulics):

  | Stage | Change | Expected current |
  |---|---|---|
  | M5-0 | Port the holder + table + mode-1 law, with the dump's inputs as compile-time constants | wire identical to r03f |
  | M5-1 | Block-0 gear 1 | A instead of B, ≈ 0 change |
  | M5-2 | Block-1 1st (A B C) | ≈ 0 |
  | M5-3 | R (A B D) | ≈ 0 |
  | M5-4 | N | −0.38 A |
  | M5-5 | Sys below 20 bar | ≈ +0.2 A |
  | M5-6 | Runtime lever / engine logic | needs the brake/speed/engine interlocks decoded first, and the transmission for any meaning |

**r03n = M5-0: THE STOCK REQUEST PRODUCER, WIRE-IDENTICAL (2026-11-16; subagent port `~/tcu_sweep/m5_0_port.md`, validated this session; built, NOT flashed, `build/r03n_IFla.bin`, app CRC `3845F36D`, app end 0x53BC8, blocks 0x40000 + 0x50000):**
- **Ported (from the bytes):**
  - producer 0xDC4C4: the Sys call (source 0x15), the WK call (source 0x16, the not-lockup path), the source-0 holder loop + 0xDCE9A;

**M5 PRODUCER INPUTS — DECODE (2026-11-17, read-only subagent, `~/tcu_sweep/m5_producer_inputs_decode.md`; key claims re-checked this session against the bytes and the dump)** [O unless marked]:
- **Gear code 3 in P is a safety-layer fault reaction, not a strategy choice.**
  - 0x89C43 is stored at 0xB9104 (`mov.b r0,@(7,r14)`, r14 = 0xFFF89C3C; re-checked) by 0xB90B2 = 0xDB63C(obj, …, code 0x89C42, …).
  - The code is 0x89C41 ← 0x13A9D4(0xFFF95858, …); result 0xFFF958A2 = **0x20** (dump, re-checked: 0x89C41..43 = 20 20 03). 0x13A9D4 returns 0/1/2 only with its fault masks (0x4B984/0x4B988) clear, else a code 0x17–0x28; 0xDB63C maps 0x20 (and 0x11) to gear 3.
  - Target path: 0x10485E → 0xD15A6 → E6A1 → 0x104A28 → E4A7 (0xFFF8E4A6[1]) → 0xD140C → 0x89C2B, which copies only while CCE4[3] ≠ 0 (dump 0, re-checked). With a fault-free code the target is `*0x89CE8` (dump 01 = gear 1, re-checked — the r03p wire); its producer 0xF7864 is not decoded.
- **The application range 0x89CDC = the park arbiter target D6D6** (dump 08, re-checked). Direction table 0x63445 indexed by it reads `01 01 01 01 01 01 00 02 00 01 01` (re-checked): 5 → forward, 6 → none, 7 → reverse, 8 → none — so the range codes are D 5, N 6, R 7, P 8. **The arbiter 0xA54A4–0xA5860 never stores 7** (no `mov #7` immediate in the range — re-checked; 5 ×4, 6 ×10, 8 ×9). So AE28 can never be reverse, and with our mode-4 arbiter (R/N/D → 6) it is always 0. **This contradicts a working car (R must exist) and blocks any P→R/P→D port.** Leads: the undecoded lever object 0xFFF8304C, or the 0xB97F8 override of the range getter (0x9B588 → 0xA7B78). [I] D6D6 may be a park-side view (P vs not-P) and the real range comes through the override.

**THE LEVER-SIM GAPS WERE THE TEENSY, NOT THE TCU OR THE SENDER (2026-11-17, zero-flash on r03r; scripts in the session scratchpad gapdiag*.py / rd_com.py)** [O; I where marked]:
- **Host side clean:** the VehicleSim send interval was 10.0 ms p50 / ≤ 10.4 ms max in every phase (lever only, and with brake + engine), with or without reads in the main thread.
- **TCU side:** the shadow recorder still showed ≈ 70 ms lever-validity losses (D673 = 02, the brake flag dropping 10 ms earlier), and the TCU's com layer (`s_st` of profile_fca, 0xFFF9E018, 18 B per message) counted **counter errors on the bus-1 messages** (0x108 4, 0x140 8, 0x2EA 21 — frames skipped) while 0x210 (no E2E) timed out at the same moments → frames missing on BOTH CAN channels at once.
- **Every gap fell where the host stopped reading the Teensy ports** (the phase boundaries of the diagnostic, the shadow-check's input()/sleep waits). [I strong] The Teensy's USB serial write blocks when the host does not drain its port, until Teensyduino's transmit timeout (~70 ms), and its single loop stops sending our frames on both channels meanwhile. This also plausibly explains r03k's "one-tick Auto-P flicker" (previously blamed on USB stalls) and shadow-check run 1's unanswered read.
- **Fix (`tools/bench/tcu_bench.py`):** `Buses.start_drain()` — a background reader thread per port into a bounded queue; `get(n).recv()` reads the queue. Enabled in `park-check` and `shadow-check`. **Re-test with the drain (lever P + brake + engine, 23 s, reads in the main thread): no lever-validity loss in the recorder, the TCU's counter errors unchanged (4/8/21), reads 120 with no failures (77–82 before, with failures).**

- **The fault manager debounces in milliseconds, not report counts [O]:** a per-group up/down timer 0xFFF8D714. A failed report adds the time since the last step (0xFFF8D89A), clamped to the fail time; **confirmation when D714 ≥ the fail time** (re-checked this session: 0x9C124 adds and clamps D714; r11 = the new D714; `cmp/ge r7,r11` at 0x9CA04 → 0x9CA70). A passed report subtracts down to 0. On confirmation the group's reaction words are OR-ed into the masks 0xFFF897D0/D8 (0x9CF9E).
- **Configs [O, re-read this session]:** group 109 U1267 (0x19331C): **fail 500 ms (+2 = 0x01F4), heal 500 ms (+4 = 0x01F4)**; group 98 P0850 (0x193190): **fail 2500 ms (0x09C4), heal 100 ms (0x0064)**. Healing: after confirmation a passed report starts a countdown at the heal time; at 0 the record goes inactive and 0xAAC0E rebuilds the masks — **group 109 leaves the masks when healed (cfg +0x1C bit 8 set); group 98 stays for the power cycle** [O subagent]. At boot 0xAAFE8 zeroes the timers/masks and the rebuild drops both groups → a stored U1267 never brings the reaction bits back at power-on [O rule; I boot ordering].

- **Port (`app/rng_shadow.c` `fm_report` …, `rng_shadow_fm_t` appended to `s_rs`, 0x180 → 0x1D4; earlier offsets unchanged):** groups 109 and 98 on the decoded timer; the tick now gets park_pair's (failed, tested) for group 98. **FAULT MANAGER — DECODE (2026-11-17, read-only subagent, `~/tcu_sweep/fault_manager_decode.md`, 669 lines incl. a 195-group table; the 0xA5 writer, its selector and the fatal handler's WDT value re-checked this session) — THREE SEPARATE "FAULT" PATHS** [O unless marked]:
- **The DTC manager = Bosch DSM** (build string "Jun  8 2017" at 0x4DE60): 195 groups × 36-byte configs, 244 DTC rows (event table 0x1942E8), two record memories (20 slots + a 10-slot ring), one UDS status byte per GROUP (0xFFF823F7 + g — not per event, correcting spec 13/the EEPROM CSV), reaction bits 0–63.
